```
1	# Todo
2	
3	This file uses [Prose form](../AGENTS.md#prose-form). It
4	contains near term tasks with a short description and
5	uses links or reference links for more details.
6	
7	## In Progress
8	
9	_No cycle currently in progress._
10	
11	## Todo
12	
13	 Entries are in **strict priority rank** — #1 highest,
14	 descending. Reprioritize by moving an entry, then
15	 `vc-x1 fix-todo --no-dry-run notes/todo.md` to renumber.
16	 The numbers are positional rank, not stable IDs — to refer
17	 to a Todo, name it by its **title** (a greppable mention;
18	 a numbered list item has no anchor to link to), not its
19	 number. Long-tail entries
20	 live in [todo-backlog.md](todo-backlog.md). Use the
21	 [Prose Form in AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#prose-form); deeper
22	 detail goes in `notes/chores/chores-NN.md` design
23	 subsections (link via `[N]` ref).
24	
25	1. Descriptor queues: carry `(pool id, buffer offset)`
26	   descriptors over the existing SPSC ring so any message
27	   travels over any queue; per-process pool registry to
28	   resolve ids
29	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#messaging-layer-pools-and-descriptor-queues).
30	2. Endpoint claims word: CAS-claimed producer/consumer roles
31	   in the ring header so a second attach/split claimant gets
32	   an error instead of silently violating SPSC; costs a
33	   layout_version bump (or spends `_pad0`)
34	   [details](ring-buffer-design.md#resolved-questions).
35	3. Typed endpoints: `Producer<T>` / `Consumer<T>` validating
36	   `T`'s geometry once at split instead of asserting on every
37	   reserve_slot [details](ring-buffer-design.md#api).
38	
39	## Ideas
40	
41	- Perf benches live in
42	  [iiac-perf](https://github.com/winksaville/iiac-perf)
43	  (sibling repo `../iiac-perf`), not here — its calibrated
44	  harness compares zc-ring against mpsc et al. directly
45	  (`zcring-1t`/`zcring-2t` mirroring `mpsc_1t`/`mpsc_2t`).
46	  An in-repo bench only if per-commit regression tracking
47	  proves necessary.
48	
49	- Study [iceoryx2](https://github.com/eclipse-iceoryx/iceoryx2)
50	  before implementing message pools — battle-tested loan/send
51	  decoupling and pool-offset machinery; how it differs from
52	  this project in
53	  [Prior art: iceoryx2](ring-buffer-design.md#prior-art-iceoryx2).
54	- `#[global_allocator]` experiment over size-class pools:
55	  GlobalAlloc is `&self` + any-thread, so it needs
56	  shared-allocation pools (phase 2 gen-tagged head) or
57	  per-thread pools with a routing layer; arbitrary `Layout`
58	  needs size-class selection + an oversize fallback. Frees
59	  from any thread are already natural (MPSC push). Classic
60	  mempool → malloc arc; measure the object-pool form in
```

```
1	//! Demo binary: both primitives working across threads,
2	//! with throughput printed — run `cargo run --release`, or
3	//! `cargo install --path . --locked` and run
4	//! `zc-ring-x1-demo`. `-V`/`--version` prints the
5	//! version-of-record so you know exactly which build you
6	//! are testing.
7	//!
8	//! - Part 1, the ring: an SPSC pair moves typed messages
9	//!   in place (reserve_slot → write → commit; reserve_slot
10	//!   → read → release), one producer thread to one
11	//!   consumer thread.
12	//! - Part 2, the pool: an allocator thread allocs and
13	//!   fills `BufSlot`s and hands them to a freer thread —
14	//!   "send" today is moving the guard (see the README's
15	//!   usage model); getting a buffer implies nothing about
16	//!   when it is sent or freed.
17	//! - The composed form — descriptors through the ring,
18	//!   payloads at rest in pool buffers — is the next cycle;
19	//!   this demo shows each primitive honestly on its own.
20	
21	use std::time::Instant;
22	
23	use zc_ring_x1::{BufSlot, CACHE_LINE_SIZE, Empty, Exhausted, Full, Pool, Ring};
24	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
25	
26	/// Messages moved per part.
27	const COUNT: u64 = 1_000_000;
28	
29	/// Ring slots / pool buffers (small on purpose: recycling
30	/// under pressure is the interesting case).
31	const DEPTH: u32 = 64;
32	
33	/// The demo message; two words so a torn write would show.
34	#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
35	#[repr(C)]
36	struct Msg {
37	    seq: u64,
38	}
39	
40	/// Region for either primitive: biggest header (ring, 4
41	/// lines) + DEPTH one-line slots/buffers.
42	#[repr(C, align(64))]
43	struct Region([u8; 4 * CACHE_LINE_SIZE + DEPTH as usize * CACHE_LINE_SIZE]);
44	
45	/// Group a count into comma-separated thousands
46	/// (`1234567` → `"1,234,567"`).
47	fn commas(n: u64) -> String {
48	    let digits = n.to_string();
49	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(digits.len() + digits.len() / 3);
50	    for (i, c) in digits.chars().enumerate() {
51	        if i > 0 && (digits.len() - i).is_multiple_of(3) {
52	            out.push(',');
53	        }
54	        out.push(c);
55	    }
56	    out
57	}
58	
59	/// Print one part's line: msgs/sec (comma-grouped) and
60	/// ns/msg from the part's elapsed seconds.
61	fn report(label: &str, secs: f64) {
62	    let rate = commas((COUNT as f64 / secs) as u64);
63	    let ns_per_msg = secs * 1e9 / COUNT as f64;
64	    println!("{label:<32} {rate:>12} msgs/sec  {ns_per_msg:>7.1} ns/msg");
65	}
66	
67	/// Move COUNT messages single thread
68	/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.
69	fn ring_one_msg_1t() -> f64 {
70	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
71	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
72	        .unwrap()
73	        .split();
74	
75	    let start = Instant::now();
76	    for i in 0..COUNT {
77	        match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
78	            Ok(mut slot) => {
79	                slot.seq = i;
80	                //println!("ring_demo_1t: producer i={i}, seq={}", slot.seq);
81	                slot.commit();
82	            }
83	            Err(Full) => {
84	                panic!("ring_demo_1t: producer Full spin_loop SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
85	            }
86	        }
87	        match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
88	            Ok(msg) => {
89	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
90	                msg.release();
91	            }
92	            Err(Empty) => {
93	                panic!("ring_demo_1t: consumer Empty spin_loop SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
94	            }
95	        }
96	    }
97	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
98	}
99	
100	/// Move COUNT messages producer-thread → consumer-thread
101	/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.
102	fn ring_one_msg_2t() -> f64 {
103	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
104	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
105	        .unwrap()
106	        .split();
107	
108	    let start = Instant::now();
109	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
110	        s.spawn(move || {
111	            for i in 0..COUNT {
112	                loop {
113	                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
114	                        Ok(mut slot) => {
115	                            slot.seq = i;
116	                            slot.commit();
117	                            break;
118	                        }
119	                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
120	                    }
121	                }
122	            }
123	        });
124	        s.spawn(move || {
125	            for i in 0..COUNT {
126	                loop {
127	                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
128	                        Ok(msg) => {
129	                            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
130	                            msg.release();
131	                            break;
132	                        }
133	                        Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
134	                    }
135	                }
136	            }
137	        });
138	    });
139	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
140	}
141	
142	/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,
143	/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std
144	/// channel); return elapsed seconds.
145	fn pool_demo() -> f64 {
146	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
147	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap();
148	
149	    let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel::<BufSlot<'_, Msg>>(DEPTH as usize);
150	    let start = Instant::now();
151	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
152	        s.spawn(move || {
153	            for i in 0..COUNT {
154	                loop {
155	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
156	                        Ok(mut buf) => {
157	                            buf.seq = i;
158	                            tx.send(buf).unwrap();
159	                            break;
160	                        }
161	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
162	                    }
163	                }
164	            }
165	        });
166	        s.spawn(move || {
167	            for i in 0..COUNT {
168	                let buf = rx.recv().unwrap();
169	                assert_eq!(buf.seq, i);
170	                buf.free();
171	            }
172	        });
173	    });
174	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
175	}
176	
177	/// Alloc → write → free COUNT messages on one thread — the
178	/// pool's own cost, no channel, no second thread; return
179	/// elapsed seconds.
180	fn pool_alloc_free_demo() -> f64 {
181	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
182	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap();
183	
184	    let start = Instant::now();
185	    for i in 0..COUNT {
186	        let mut buf = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap();
187	        buf.seq = i;
188	        std::hint::black_box(buf.seq);
189	        buf.free();
190	    }
191	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
192	}
193	
194	/// The same loop through the global allocator (Box::new →
195	/// write → drop) for comparison; return elapsed seconds.
196	fn global_alloc_free_demo() -> f64 {
197	    let start = Instant::now();
198	    for i in 0..COUNT {
199	        let mut buf = std::hint::black_box(Box::new(Msg { seq: 0 }));
200	        buf.seq = i;
201	        std::hint::black_box(buf.seq);
202	        drop(buf);
203	    }
204	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
205	}
206	
207	/// Run both parts and print their throughput; `-V` /
208	/// `--version` prints the version-of-record and exits.
209	fn main() {
210	    let banner = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
211	    println!("{banner}");
212	    if std::env::args().any(|a| a == "-V" || a == "--version") {
213	        return;
214	    }
215	    println!("demo: {} messages each, depth {DEPTH}", commas(COUNT));
216	    report("ring_one_msg_1t (SPSC, in-place):", ring_one_msg_1t());
217	    report("ring_one_msg_2t (SPSC, in-place):", ring_one_msg_2t());
218	    report("pool (alloc here -> free there):", pool_demo());
219	    report("pool (alloc/free, 1 thread):", pool_alloc_free_demo());
220	    report(
221	        "global (Box::new/drop, 1 thread):",
222	        global_alloc_free_demo(),
223	    );
224	    println!("(composed descriptor flow arrives with the descriptor-queue cycle)");
225	}
226	
```

> AGENT

Clippy is clean. Here's the review of the uncommitted demo changes.

**What changed:** `Msg` shrank from two words (`seq` + `val`) to one; the old `ring_demo` was renamed `ring_one_msg_2t`; a new single-threaded `ring_one_msg_1t` variant was added and reported first, so the output now separates the ring's own cost from the cross-thread cost — a nice parallel to the existing `pool_demo` / `pool_alloc_free_demo` split.

Findings, roughly in order of importance:

1. **Stale `Msg` doc comment** (`src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs:33`): "two words so a torn write would show" — the struct is now one word, so the comment is wrong, and the torn-write-detection property it describes is actually gone. If dropping `val` was deliberate (smaller message = cleaner perf number), the doc should say what the one-word message is for instead; if the torn-write canary was still wanted, this is a semantic regression in the 2t test, not just a doc nit.
2. **Copy-paste panic messages in the new 1t function** (lines 84, 93): they say `ring_demo_1t` (a name that never existed — the function is `ring_one_msg_1t`) and "spin_loop SHOULD NOT HAPPEN" though this function has no spin loop. Something like `"ring_one_msg_1t: Full impossible: every produce is followed by a consume"` would say why it can't happen.
3. **Commented-out debug `println!`** at line 80 — leftover scaffolding; should be deleted before commit.
4. **Report columns now misalign**: `report` pads labels to 32 (`{label:<32}` at line 64), but both new ring labels — `"ring_one_msg_1t (SPSC, in-place):"` — are 33 characters, so the ring lines' numbers shift one column right of the pool/global lines. Either widen the pad or shorten the labels.
5. **Minor**: the new `Ring::init(...).unwrap()` at line 72 has no `// OK:` justification comment (AGENTS.md requires one on non-test `unwrap`; the pre-existing demo unwraps also lack them, so this just continues that drift). And the module doc's Part-1 bullet (lines 8–11) still describes the ring demo as strictly "one producer thread to one consumer thread" — now only true of the 2t variant.

None of these block anything functionally — clippy and the build are clean. Want me to apply the fixes (doc comment, panic messages, dead println, label width), or were some of these intentional?

> AGENT

All three fixes are in, and fmt/clippy/run are clean:

- `Msg` doc now says it's one word carrying the sequence number the consumer asserts (the torn-write line is gone).
- `report` pads labels to 33 to match the new longest label — the run above shows all five columns aligned.
- The new `Ring::init(...).unwrap()` carries `// OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots`, and the module doc's Part-1 bullet now describes both the one-thread and two-thread ring variants.

One observation from the run worth talking about: single-thread ring is ~2.7 ns/msg vs ~69 ns/msg cross-thread — the 2t number is dominated by cache-line ping-pong between cores, not ring overhead. Ready to talk whenever you are.

> AGENT

Nice — the numbers line up with mine except one interesting divergence: your 2t ring run is 16.7 ns/msg where mine was 69.1, with the 1t number identical (2.7 ns). Same binary, so that's not code — it's thread placement. The 3900X has two CCDs with four separate L3 complexes (CCXs); the demo spawns two scoped threads and lets the scheduler place them. When producer and consumer land on the same CCX the head/tail/slot cache lines bounce through a shared L3 and you get ~17 ns; when they land on different CCDs the transfer goes over the Infinity Fabric and it balloons toward ~70. We think that's the whole story — you'd see it flip run to run, and pinning with `taskset -c 0,1` vs `taskset -c 0,12` would reproduce both ends deterministically if you want to confirm.

The rest of the story the numbers tell:

- **1t ring at 2.7 ns** is the ring's own instruction cost — everything stays in L1, no coherence traffic. That's the honest "primitive overhead" number.
- **pool 2t at 282 ns** is not the pool — it's the `std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel` carrying the guards, plus the same cross-core traffic. The pool's own cost is the 1t line: 9.7 ns per alloc+free.
- **pool 1t (9.7) vs global (7.1)**: Box::new/drop on a hot loop hits malloc's thread-local fast path, which is very good; the pool isn't ahead in this shape. The pool's value is the cross-thread/shared-memory story, not beating thread-local malloc on a single thread.

One thought for the demo's honesty: since 2t placement makes the number swing 4× between runs, it might be worth either noting that in the demo's doc comment, or eventually pinning threads — though per your Ideas note, real benchmarking belongs in iiac-perf with its calibrated harness, so a doc note may be all this demo wants.

What did you want to dig into?

> DEVELOPER

Yea we should probably have two tests one pined on the same core another on different cores

What would you like to clarify about the variants or the pinning approach?

> DEVELOPER

My understanding was each physical core has to logical cores there there is a performance difference if the two threads are on the same physical core compared to if they are on two different physical cores.

> DEVELOPER

My understanding was each physical core has two logical cores and that there is a performance difference if the two threads are on two logical cores on the same physical core compared to if they are on two threads are on two different physical cores.

> AGENT

Your understanding is right, and it's the core of the experiment: two SMT siblings share their physical core's L1/L2 cache, so the producer→consumer cache-line handoff is nearly free, while two different physical cores must move the lines through L3 or the fabric. (On your 3900X "different physical cores" has a further split — same L3 complex vs. different CCD — which is why your unpinned runs wander between ~17 and ~70 ns. I'll have the "different cores" variant prefer a core outside cpu0's L3 group so it shows the far end deterministically.)

I'll add: `libc` (Linux-only, for `sched_setaffinity`), topology discovery from `/sys` (SMT sibling pair + a far core), and parameterize `ring_one_msg_2t` with an optional pin pair — keeping the unpinned run as-is.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Print one part's line: msgs/sec (comma-grouped) and\n/// ns/msg from the part's elapsed seconds.\nfn report(label: &str, secs: f64) {\n    let rate = commas((COUNT as f64 / secs) as u64);\n    let ns_per_msg = secs * 1e9 / COUNT as f64;\n    println!(\"{label:<33} {rate:>12} msgs/sec  {ns_per_msg:>7.1} ns/msg\");\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Print one part's line: msgs/sec (comma-grouped) and\n/// ns/msg from the part's elapsed seconds.\nfn report(label: &str, secs: f64) {\n    let rate = commas((COUNT as f64 / secs) as u64);\n    let ns_per_msg = secs * 1e9 / COUNT as f64;\n    println!(\"{label:<33} {rate:>12} msgs/sec  {ns_per_msg:>7.1} ns/msg\");\n}\n\n/// Parse a /sys cpu-list string (\"0,12\" or \"0-2,6\") into cpu\n/// numbers; malformed pieces are skipped.\nfn parse_cpu_list(s: &str) -> Vec<usize> {\n    let mut out = Vec::new();\n    for part in s.trim().split(',') {\n        match part.split_once('-') {\n            Some((lo, hi)) => {\n                if let (Ok(lo), Ok(hi)) = (lo.parse::<usize>(), hi.parse::<usize>()) {\n                    out.extend(lo..=hi);\n                }\n            }\n            None => {\n                if let Ok(n) = part.parse() {\n                    out.push(n);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    out\n}\n\n/// Discover two cpu pairs for the pinned ring runs from\n/// /sys/devices/system/cpu; `None` when the machine lacks the\n/// shape (or not Linux).\n///\n/// - `.0` — same physical core: cpu0 and its SMT sibling\n///   (shared L1/L2, the cheapest handoff).\n/// - `.1` — different physical cores: cpu0 and a core outside\n///   cpu0's L3 group when one exists (the farthest handoff),\n///   else any non-sibling core.\n#[cfg(target_os = \"linux\")]\nfn discover_pin_pairs() -> (Option<(usize, usize)>, Option<(usize, usize)>) {\n    let read = |path: &str| std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok();\n    let siblings = read(\"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list\")\n        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))\n        .unwrap_or_default();\n    let smt = (siblings.len() >= 2).then(|| (siblings[0], siblings[1]));\n    let online = read(\"/sys/devices/system/cpu/online\")\n        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))\n        .unwrap_or_default();\n    let l3 = read(\"/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/cache/index3/shared_cpu_list\")\n        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))\n        .unwrap_or_else(|| siblings.clone());\n    let far = online\n        .iter()\n        .copied()\n        .find(|c| !l3.contains(c))\n        .or_else(|| online.iter().copied().find(|c| *c != 0 && !siblings.contains(c)));\n    (smt, far.map(|c| (0, c)))\n}\n\n/// Non-Linux stub: no pinned runs.\n#[cfg(not(target_os = \"linux\"))]\nfn discover_pin_pairs() -> (Option<(usize, usize)>, Option<(usize, usize)>) {\n    (None, None)\n}\n\n/// Pin the calling thread to `cpu` via sched_setaffinity;\n/// panics on failure (a demo run with a silently ignored pin\n/// would report a mislabeled number).\n#[cfg(target_os = \"linux\")]\nfn pin_to_cpu(cpu: usize) {\n    // SAFETY: cpu_set_t is a plain bitmask; CPU_ZERO/CPU_SET\n    // initialize it fully before sched_setaffinity reads it.\n    unsafe {\n        let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = std::mem::zeroed();\n        libc::CPU_ZERO(&mut set);\n        libc::CPU_SET(cpu, &mut set);\n        let rc = libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &set);\n        assert_eq!(rc, 0, \"sched_setaffinity({cpu}) failed\");\n    }\n}\n\n/// Non-Linux stub: never called (discover_pin_pairs returns\n/// no pairs), present so the demo compiles everywhere.\n#[cfg(not(target_os = \"linux\"))]\nfn pin_to_cpu(_cpu: usize) {}"
}
```

```
93	/// Discover two cpu pairs for the pinned ring runs from
94	/// /sys/devices/system/cpu; `None` when the machine lacks the
95	/// shape (or not Linux).
96	///
97	/// - `.0` — same physical core: cpu0 and its SMT sibling
98	///   (shared L1/L2, the cheapest handoff).
99	/// - `.1` — different physical cores: cpu0 and a core outside
100	///   cpu0's L3 group when one exists (the farthest handoff),
101	///   else any non-sibling core.
102	#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
103	fn discover_pin_pairs() -> (Option<(usize, usize)>, Option<(usize, usize)>) {
104	    let read = |path: &str| std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok();
105	    let siblings = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list")
106	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
107	        .unwrap_or_default();
108	    let smt = (siblings.len() >= 2).then(|| (siblings[0], siblings[1]));
109	    let online = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/online")
110	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
111	        .unwrap_or_default();
112	    let l3 = read("/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/cache/index3/shared_cpu_list")
113	        .map(|s| parse_cpu_list(&s))
114	        .unwrap_or_else(|| siblings.clone());
115	    let far = online
116	        .iter()
117	        .copied()
118	        .find(|c| !l3.contains(c))
119	        .or_else(|| {
120	            online
121	                .iter()
122	                .copied()
123	                .find(|c| *c != 0 && !siblings.contains(c))
124	        });
125	    (smt, far.map(|c| (0, c)))
126	}
127

129	#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
130	fn discover_pin_pairs() -> (Option<(usize, usize)>, Option<(usize, usize)>) {
131	    (None, None)
132	}
```

> AGENT

Both pinned variants are in and the run shows exactly the effect you were after:

```
ring_one_msg_1t (SPSC, in-place):  363,906,713 msgs/sec      2.7 ns/msg
ring_one_msg_2t (unpinned):         18,923,563 msgs/sec     52.8 ns/msg
ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):  130,695,531 msgs/sec      7.7 ns/msg
ring_one_msg_2t (diff cores 0+3):    5,198,587 msgs/sec    192.4 ns/msg
```

Same physical core (cpu0 + its SMT sibling cpu12, shared L1/L2) runs at 7.7 ns/msg — 25× faster than different physical cores at 192 ns/msg. The unpinned number lands in between, which is the scheduler wandering. Note the "diff cores" pair deliberately picks a core **outside cpu0's L3 group** (on the 3900X, cpu0's L3 covers cores 0–2, so it chose cpu3 in the next CCX) — that's the far end of the ladder; a different-core-same-L3 pair would land somewhere in the middle.

What landed:

- `Cargo.toml`: `libc` as a Linux-only target dependency for `sched_setaffinity`.
- `discover_pin_pairs()` reads `/sys/devices/system/cpu` topology at runtime — SMT sibling pair from `thread_siblings_list`, far pair preferring a cpu outside cpu0's `cache/index3/shared_cpu_list` — so it adapts to any Linux box and each pinned run prints a "skipped" line instead when the machine lacks the shape (single-core, SMT off).
- `ring_one_msg_2t(pin: PinPair)` — each thread pins itself when a pair is given; `None` keeps the original unpinned behavior. `pin_to_cpu` panics on failure so a mislabeled number can't slip through.
- Non-Linux stubs so the demo still compiles elsewhere (pinned runs just skip).
- fmt, clippy, and tests are all clean (a `PinPair` type alias resolved a `type_complexity` warning).

One caveat worth stating: 192 ns for cross-L3 is notably worse than your earlier ~70 ns unpinned worst case. We think the fixed 0+3 pairing plus both threads spinning hard on the same lines produces a steadier, fuller ping-pong than scheduler-placed threads that occasionally migrate — but that's inference, not measured. Worth watching across a few runs on your side.

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking we should change the names in the demo to ring_one_msg_xxx to spsc_ring_one_msg_xxx, and remove the SPSC in _1t, thoughts

> AGENT

Agree with the direction — with the pinned variants, the parenthetical is doing run-condition work ("unpinned", "same core 0+12"), so the queue discipline belongs in the name, not the label. `spsc_ring_one_msg_1t` also sets up a clean contrast for later variants (the pool's free path is already MPSC-shaped, and descriptor queues will reuse the SPSC ring — names like `spsc_ring_desc_...` extend naturally). And dropping "(SPSC, in-place)" from `_1t` is right once the name carries it: `_2t` already dropped it for the pin condition, so `_1t` keeping it was asymmetric anyway.

One consequence to decide before I apply it: label width. The current pad is 33; the new longest label — `spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):` — is 39 characters, which puts the full line at ~79 columns. Two ways to go:

1. **Widen the pad to 39** and keep labels verbatim (`spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):`). Simple, but the lines nearly fill 80 columns and a 2-digit cpu pair (e.g. `10+22` on another box) would push past.
2. **Shorten the parentheticals** — `(smt 0+12)` / `(far 0+3)` / `(unpinned)` — keeping the pad around 33–34 with headroom.

I'd lean to option 1 for readability ("same core" / "diff cores" say what "smt"/"far" make the reader decode), accepting the wide lines. Want me to go ahead with that, or do you prefer the terse labels?

> DEVELOPER

go with option 1

```
295	/// The same loop through the global allocator (Box::new →
296	/// write → drop) for comparison; return elapsed seconds.
297	fn global_alloc_free_demo() -> f64 {
298	    let start = Instant::now();
299	    for i in 0..COUNT {
300	        let mut buf = std::hint::black_box(Box::new(Msg { seq: 0 }));
301	        buf.seq = i;
302	        std::hint::black_box(buf.seq);
303	        drop(buf);
304	    }
305	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
306	}

318	    report("ring_one_msg_2t (unpinned):", ring_one_msg_2t(None));

323	            ring_one_msg_2t(smt),
324	        ),
325	        None => println!("ring_one_msg_2t (same core):      skipped, no SMT sibling found"),
326	    }
327	    match far {
328	        Some((p, c)) => report(
329	            &format!("ring_one_msg_2t (diff cores {p}+{c}):"),
330	            ring_one_msg_2t(far),
331	        ),

336	    report(
337	        "global (Box::new/drop, 1 thread):",

339	    );

341	}
```

> AGENT

Pinning the 1t run to cpu 0 has one subtlety: `sched_setaffinity` on the main thread would be inherited by every thread spawned afterward — the pool demos' threads would all get squeezed onto cpu 0. So I'll run the 1t loop in a short-lived scoped thread that pins itself, same shape as the 2t runs.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "/// Move COUNT messages single thread\n/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.\nfn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    for i in 0..COUNT {\n        match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n            Ok(mut slot) => {\n                slot.seq = i;\n                slot.commit();\n            }\n            Err(Full) => {\n                panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: producer Full spin_loop SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n            }\n        }\n        match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n            Ok(msg) => {\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n            Err(Empty) => {\n                panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: consumer Empty spin_loop SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Move COUNT messages single thread through the ring,\n/// pinned to cpu 0 for consistency with the pinned 2t runs;\n/// return elapsed seconds.\n///\n/// The loop runs in a scoped thread rather than pinning the\n/// main thread: spawned threads inherit the main thread's\n/// affinity mask, which would squeeze every later part onto\n/// cpu 0.\nfn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n                    Ok(mut slot) => {\n                        slot.seq = i;\n                        slot.commit();\n                    }\n                    Err(Full) => {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n                    }\n                }\n                match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {\n                    Ok(msg) => {\n                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                        msg.release();\n                    }\n                    Err(Empty) => {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Done — the 1t run is now pinned to cpu 0 and labeled `(core 0)`, so the whole ring ladder reads consistently: `core 0` → `same core 0+12` → `diff cores 0+3`. The number is unchanged (~2.8 ns/msg), as expected — it was already core-local, the pin just makes it deterministic.

Two details from the change:

- The 1t loop now runs in a scoped thread that pins itself, rather than pinning the main thread — spawned threads inherit the parent's affinity mask, so pinning main would have silently squeezed the pool demos onto cpu 0 too.
- While touching the panic messages I also dropped the leftover "spin_loop" word from them (`producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN`) — there's no spin loop in the 1t function.

fmt and clippy are clean.

> DEVELOPER

We have pool_demo is std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t, am I correct?

> AGENT

Essentially yes. `pool_demo` is: alloc a pool buffer on thread A, move its `BufSlot` guard across a `std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel`, assert and free on thread B — so in the naming scheme we just established, the transport is std's mpsc channel, the cargo is one pool message, two threads: `std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t` describes it accurately.

Two nuances worth noting before you commit to the name:

- **It's std's mpsc used SPSC.** The channel type is multi-producer, but the demo has exactly one sender and one receiver — the same discipline as the ring runs. So the name is honest about the *mechanism* (which is what determines the cost — std's channel pays for its MPSC generality either way), but the *usage* is SPSC. That's actually the interesting comparison: SPSC ring at ~7–200 ns depending on placement vs. std's channel at ~280 ns doing the same one-producer-one-consumer job.
- **It measures pool + channel together.** The line is "alloc here → free there," so ~280 ns is channel transport plus the pool's cross-thread free path, not the channel alone. The pool's own cost is isolated by `pool_alloc_free_demo` (9.6 ns).

If the goal is renaming for consistency, the set would land as something like `std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t` (pool_demo), `pool_alloc_free_1t` (pool_alloc_free_demo), `global_alloc_free_1t` (global_alloc_free_demo) — with labels shrinking the same way the ring ones did. Want me to do that?

> AGENT

Both `_1t` runs are now pinned to cpu 0 — each runs its loop in a scoped thread that pins itself (same pattern as the ring 1t, keeping the main thread's affinity untouched), and the labels read `(core 0)`:

```
spsc_ring_one_msg_1t (core 0):           346,188,105 msgs/sec      2.9 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (unpinned):          17,474,203 msgs/sec     57.2 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):    85,922,389 msgs/sec     11.6 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (diff cores 0+3):     5,344,326 msgs/sec    187.1 ns/msg
std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (unpinned):       3,743,798 msgs/sec    267.1 ns/msg
pool_alloc_free_1t (core 0):             102,189,804 msgs/sec      9.8 ns/msg
global_alloc_free_1t (core 0):           143,517,260 msgs/sec      7.0 ns/msg
```

Numbers are unchanged from their unpinned values, as expected — single-threaded loops were already core-local — but now every line states its placement explicitly, and the two new unwraps in `pool_alloc_free_1t` picked up `// OK:` justifications. fmt and clippy are clean.

The demo has accumulated a fair amount of change since `044e346` (pinning infrastructure, the `libc` dep, all the renames). Ready to fold into a commit whenever you want — per the cycle protocol that'd start a cycle, so say the word.

```
255	/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,
256	/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std
257	/// channel); return elapsed seconds.
258	fn std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t() -> f64 {
259	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
260	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap();
261	
262	    let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel::<BufSlot<'_, Msg>>(DEPTH as usize);
263	    let start = Instant::now();
264	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
265	        s.spawn(move || {
266	            for i in 0..COUNT {
267	                loop {
268	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
269	                        Ok(mut buf) => {
270	                            buf.seq = i;
271	                            tx.send(buf).unwrap();
272	                            break;
273	                        }
274	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
275	                    }
276	                }
277	            }
278	        });
279	        s.spawn(move || {
280	            for i in 0..COUNT {
281	                let buf = rx.recv().unwrap();
282	                assert_eq!(buf.seq, i);
283	                buf.free();
284	            }
285	        });
286	    });
287	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
288	}
289	
290	/// Alloc → write → free COUNT messages on one thread pinned
291	/// to cpu 0 — the pool's own cost, no channel, no second
292	/// thread; return elapsed seconds.
293	///
294	/// Runs in a scoped thread like the other pinned parts, so
295	/// the main thread's affinity stays untouched.
296	fn pool_alloc_free_1t() -> f64 {
297	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
298	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
299
```

> AGENT

The pinned `std_mpsc` variants are in — fmt and clippy clean:

```
spsc_ring_one_msg_1t (core 0):               351,056,416 msgs/sec      2.8 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (unpinned):              24,034,656 msgs/sec     41.6 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):        87,372,444 msgs/sec     11.4 ns/msg
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (diff cores 0+3):         5,387,859 msgs/sec    185.6 ns/msg
std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (unpinned):           4,920,831 msgs/sec    203.2 ns/msg
std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (same core 0+12):    15,932,221 msgs/sec     62.8 ns/msg
std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t (diff cores 0+3):     3,243,391 msgs/sec    308.3 ns/msg
pool_alloc_free_1t (core 0):                  99,463,266 msgs/sec     10.1 ns/msg
global_alloc_free_1t (core 0):               134,746,609 msgs/sec      7.4 ns/msg
```

`std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_2t` now takes the same `PinPair` parameter as the ring 2t (allocator thread gets the first cpu, freer the second) and `main` reuses the pairs from `discover_pin_pairs`, so both 2t families run the identical unpinned / same-core / diff-cores ladder. The pad widened to 43 for the new longest label, and the skipped-fallback lines were re-padded to match.

The comparison now reads nicely: at every placement the ring beats std's channel + pool by roughly 2–5× (same core 11.4 vs 62.8, diff cores 185.6 vs 308.3), and the gap narrows as cache-line transfer cost dominates both — which is what you'd expect once the interconnect, not the queue discipline, is the bottleneck.

```
280	    ///
281	    /// - Returns the atomic cell, not the value; symmetric
282	    ///   with the header's `first_free_idx`.
283	    /// - Callers pass `idx < buf_count` (validated pops /
284	    ///   init's linking loop), keeping the deref in bounds.
285	    fn next_buf_idx(&self, idx: u32) -> &AtomicU32 {
286	        let p = self.buf_ptr(idx) as *const AtomicU32;
287	        // SAFETY: idx < buf_count keeps the buffer in the
288	        // region validated at init/attach; the base is
289	        // cache-line aligned and buf_size is a line multiple,
290	        // so the first word is 4-aligned; all peers access it
291	        // as an atomic.
292	        unsafe { &*p }
293	    }
294	
295	    /// Pointer to buffer `idx`; callers pass
296	    /// `idx < buf_count`.
297	    fn buf_ptr(&self, idx: u32) -> *mut u8 {
298	        // SAFETY: idx < buf_count, so the offset stays inside
299	        // the buffer array validated at init/attach.
300	        unsafe { self.bufs.add(idx as usize * self.buf_size as usize) }
301	    }
302	}
303	
304	/// An allocated buffer, owned until [`free`](BufSlot::free):
305	/// `DerefMut` to use it as a `T` in place.
306	///
307	/// - Does not borrow the [`Pool`] — hold any number, as long
308	///   as you like; getting a buffer implies nothing about
309	///   when (or whether) it is sent or freed.
310	/// - Dropping without `free` leaks the buffer until the pool
311	///   is re-initialized (the abandon analog of the ring's
312	///   guards, with the opposite consequence).
313	pub struct BufSlot<'p, T> {
314	    /// The pool's control block (for the free CAS).
315	    header: &'p PoolHeader,
316	    /// Base of the owned buffer. Raw, and references are
317	    /// minted per access — same aliasing rationale as the
318	    /// ring guards.
319	    buf: *mut u8,
320	    /// This buffer's index (the value free pushes).
321	    idx: u32,
322	    /// The guard acts as a `&mut T` into the region.
323	    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,
324	}
325	
326	// SAFETY: the guard owns its buffer exclusively (the pop
327	// removed it from every shared structure); free's CAS is the
328	// only shared-state touch and is properly ordered.
329	unsafe impl<T: Send> Send for BufSlot<'_, T> {}
330	
331	impl<T> Deref for BufSlot<'_, T> {
332	    type Target = T;
333	    /// Read access to the buffer as a `T`.
334	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
335	        // SAFETY: buf is in-bounds and cache-line aligned
336	        // (validated geometry), any byte pattern is a valid T
337	        // (FromBytes bound at alloc), and the pop gave this
338	        // guard sole ownership until free.
339	        unsafe { &*(self.buf as *const T) }
340	    }
341	}
342	
343	impl<T> DerefMut for BufSlot<'_, T> {
344	    /// Write access to the buffer as a `T`.
345	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
346	        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of
347	        // the minted reference.
348	        unsafe { &mut *(self.buf as *mut T) }
349	    }
350	}
351	
352	impl<T> BufSlot<'_, T> {
353	    /// Push the buffer back onto the pool's free-stack.
354	    ///
355	    /// - Any holder may free — this is the free-stack's MPSC
356	    ///   push side; only allocation is single-popper.
357	    /// - The CAS retries only when another free (or the
358	    ///   allocator's pop) moves the head first.
359	    pub fn free(self) {
360	        // The buffer's first word becomes its next-link again.
361	        let link = self.buf as *const AtomicU32;
362	        // SAFETY: buf is in-bounds and 4-aligned (cache-line
363	        // aligned base); the guard still owns the buffer, and
364	        // all peers access this word as an atomic.
365	        let link = unsafe { &*link };
366	        loop {
367	            let head = self.header.first_free_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
368	            link.store(head, Ordering::Relaxed);
369	            // Release pairs with alloc's Acquire loads: the
370	            // popper that sees idx also sees the link store
371	            // above (and this holder's last buffer writes).
372	            if self
373	                .header
374	                .first_free_idx
375	                .compare_exchange(head, self.idx, Ordering::Release, Ordering::Relaxed)
376	                .is_ok()
377	            {
378	                return;
379	            }
```

```
325	## Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues
326	
327	Design for the layer above the ring. The pool half is
328	as-built as of the 0.6.0 cycle (`src/pool.rs`: layout,
329	init/attach, alloc/free); descriptor queues and provenance
330	remain design. The sections above are the as-built record of
331	the ring itself.
332	
333	The ring's `reserve_slot` fuses three acts that a messaging
334	system needs separated: it allocates message memory (the slot),
335	assigns queue position (the reservation is the ring head), and
336	opens a publication window (the guard exclusively borrows the
337	endpoint, blocking all other sends). Getting a message must not
338	imply sending it now — or ever. The fix is a layer, not a ring
339	rework: pools own message memory, queues carry small
340	descriptors, and the existing ring is the unchanged primitive
341	underneath (reserve-to-commit becomes a momentary act, where
342	the fusion is harmless).
343	
344	### Layer requirements
345	
346	- **Decoupled get/hold/send/free** — allocate a message, hold
347	  it indefinitely, send it over any queue later, forward it,
348	  or free it unsent.
349	- **Any quantity of queues and pools** in a system, and any
350	  message can travel over any queue.
351	- **Inter- and intra-process** — processes share queue ends,
352	  and message payloads zero-copy across process boundaries.
353	  The embedded single-address-space case is the degenerate
354	  mapping where all peers share one base.
355	- **Heterogeneous pools** — pools differ arbitrarily in buffer
356	  size and count; each meets the alignment constraints and
357	  self-describes its geometry.
358	- **Phasing** — queues are SPSC rings now, an MPSC ring is a
359	  future sibling primitive; pools have a single allocator
360	  now, shared allocation eventually.
361	
362	### Provenance and descriptors
363	
364	- **Offsets only, everywhere** — different per-process
365	  mappings mean no pointers in any shared structure; this
366	  also serves the single-address-space case unchanged.
367	- **Message provenance** — every buffer begins with a small
368	  header naming its pool `(pool id, offset)`; whoever holds a
369	  message last must free it to the right pool, so provenance
370	  travels with the message.
371	- **Descriptors** — a queue entry is a uniform small
372	  `(pool id, buffer offset)` regardless of message size; the
373	  receiver resolves the pool id and learns geometry from the
374	  pool's own header.
375	- **Pool self-description** — each pool region starts with a
376	  header (magic, layout version, buffer size, count,
377	  alignment), attach-validated exactly like the ring header.
378	- **Pool-id resolution** — each process keeps a local registry
379	  mapping pool id to its own mapping of that pool's region.
380	  "Any message over any queue" carries the precondition that
381	  both endpoints have the message's pool mapped.
382	- **Trust** — offsets and indices arriving through shared
383	  memory are untrusted: bounds- and alignment-checked against
384	  the receiver's own view before use, same discipline as the
385	  ring's geometry snapshot.
386	
```

```
106	impl<'a> Pool<'a> {
107	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the pool over it,
108	    /// with every buffer on the free-stack.
109	    ///
110	    /// - `buf_size` — bytes per buffer, a [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]
111	    ///   multiple.
112	    /// - `buf_count` — number of buffers, nonzero and
113	    ///   `< u32::MAX`.
114	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE_SIZE`]-aligned and at
115	    ///   least `size_of::<PoolHeader>() + buf_count *
116	    ///   buf_size` bytes.
117	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], buf_size: u32, buf_count: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
118	        validate_pool_geometry(buf_size, buf_count)?;
119	        let len = region.len();
120	        // Taken exactly once — same Stacked Borrows retag
121	        // hazard as Ring::init.
122	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
123	        let header = pool_header_ptr(base, len)?;
124	        if (len as u64) < pool_region_size(buf_size, buf_count) {
125	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
126	        }
127	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the PoolHeader checked
128	        // by pool_header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed
129	        // for 'a; any byte pattern is a valid PoolHeader
130	        // (all-atomic fields, plain-byte padding).
131	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
132	        header
133	            .layout_version
134	            .store(POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
135	        header.buf_size.store(buf_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
136	        header.buf_count.store(buf_count, Ordering::Relaxed);
137	        header
138	            .cache_line_size
139	            .store(CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
140	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + buffers.
141	        let bufs = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<PoolHeader>()) };
142	        let pool = Pool {
143	            header,
144	            bufs,
145	            buf_size,
146	            buf_count,
147	            _region: PhantomData,
148	        };
149	        // Link every buffer into the free-stack: i -> i + 1,
150	        // last -> NIL, head -> 0.
151	        for i in 0..buf_count {
152	            let next = if i + 1 == buf_count { NIL } else { i + 1 };
153	            pool.next_buf_idx(i).store(next, Ordering::Relaxed);
154	        }
155	        pool.header.first_free_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
156	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region
157	        // must never observe the magic before the geometry it
158	        // validates (same handshake as Ring::init).
159	        pool.header.magic.store(POOL_MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
160	        Ok(pool)
161	    }
162	
163	    /// Attach to a pool region another process (or an earlier
164	    /// call) already initialized, validating its header.
165	    ///
166	    /// # Safety
167	    ///
168	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
169	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
170	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
171	    /// - At most one attached handle acts as the pool's
172	    ///   allocator (single-popper contract); any handle may
173	    ///   free.
174	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
175	        let header = pool_header_ptr(region, len)?;
176	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the PoolHeader checked
177	        // by pool_header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is
178	        // live and shared.
179	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
180	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic.
181	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != POOL_MAGIC {
182	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
183	        }
184	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION {
185	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
186	        }
187	        if header.cache_line_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32 {
188	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
189	        }
190	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read.
191	        let buf_size = header.buf_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
192	        let buf_count = header.buf_count.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
193	        validate_pool_geometry(buf_size, buf_count)?;
194	        if (len as u64) < pool_region_size(buf_size, buf_count) {
195	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
196	        }
197	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + buffers.
198	        let bufs = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<PoolHeader>()) };
199	        Ok(Pool {
200	            header,
201	            bufs,
202	            buf_size,
203	            buf_count,
204	            _region: PhantomData,
205	        })
206	    }
207	
208	    /// Pop the first free buffer off the free-stack as an
209	    /// owned [`BufSlot`], or [`Exhausted`].
210	    ///
211	    /// - `&mut self` is the in-process single-popper token;
212	    ///   cross-process, at most one attached handle allocates
213	    ///   (see [`Pool::attach`]). The guard does **not** borrow
214	    ///   the pool — any number of allocated buffers may be
215	    ///   live at once; per-buffer exclusivity comes from the
216	    ///   free-stack protocol (each pop yields a distinct
217	    ///   index), not the borrow checker.
218	    /// - Validated pop: the head and its next-link live in
219	    ///   peer-writable memory, so both are bounds-checked;
220	    ///   corruption degrades to `Exhausted` (mirroring the
221	    ///   ring's fail-toward-`Full` occupancy check), never to
222	    ///   an out-of-bounds buffer.
223	    /// - A racing free moves the head and the CAS retries;
224	    ///   with a single popper an in-flight head node cannot be
225	    ///   removed underneath us, so there is no ABA hazard.
226	    /// - `T` geometry is asserted on each call (a mismatch is
227	    ///   a programming error, so it panics — same doctrine as
228	    ///   `reserve_slot`). A zero-sized `T` is legal and still
229	    ///   consumes a whole buffer: the buffer is the
230	    ///   allocation granule.
231	    pub fn alloc<T>(&mut self) -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, Exhausted>
232	    where
233	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
234	    {
235	        check_type::<T>(self.buf_size);
```

> AGENT

Good news and bad news. I checked the pool API (`src/pool.rs`) — the answer is: **not possible with the current public API, but it's two small methods away**, and those two methods are a deliberately scoped slice of Todo #1 (descriptor queues).

What the composed flow needs, and what exists:

- **A second pool handle on the consumer thread** — ✅ exists. `Pool::attach` (`src/pool.rs:174`) gives another handle over the same region, and the contract already allows it: one handle allocates, any handle frees.
- **Getting a descriptor out of a `BufSlot`** — ❌ missing. The buffer index (`idx`, `src/pool.rs:321`) is private, and the guard's only exits are `free()` or leak. There's no `into_idx(self) -> u32` that says "I'm converting my ownership into a descriptor."
- **Rebuilding a `BufSlot` from a descriptor** — ❌ missing. The attached consumer handle has no `from_idx(idx) -> BufSlot` — no way to mint a guard (or even a `&T`) from an index it received.

So the demo shape would be: producer `pool.alloc::<Msg>()` → write `seq` → `slot.into_idx()` → send the `u32` through the SPSC ring as the message; consumer reads the `u32` from the ring → `pool.from_idx::<Msg>(idx)` → assert → `free()`. The ring's commit/reserve Release/Acquire already transfers visibility of the payload writes, so the ordering story is sound; `from_idx` bounds-checks the index and is `unsafe` only because ownership transfer ("this idx came from `into_idx` and wasn't duplicated") can't be proven by types — exactly the trust posture the design doc already prescribes for descriptors arriving through shared memory.

Without those methods there's no honest version: you could FIFO the guards through a `VecDeque` (1t) or a std channel (2t) alongside the ring, but then the side channel is doing the real ownership transport and the ring is decoration.

Note this is the intentionally-small version of Todo #1: full descriptor queues carry `(pool id, buffer offset)` with a per-process pool registry and provenance headers, so any message travels over any queue. The demo needs none of that — one pool, one ring, bare `u32` index — and building the small slice first would validate the mechanics the full design rests on.

Since this means library changes, it's a new cycle. Want me to open one — roughly: `-1` add `BufSlot::into_idx` + `Pool::from_idx` with protocol tests, `-2` the two demo functions (`spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t/2t` with the same pin ladder), close-out — or would you rather go straight at the full Todo #1 design instead?
