{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/registry.rs",
  "content": "//! Descriptors and the per-process pool registry — the\n//! resolution half of the messaging layer: a [`Desc`] names a\n//! buffer as `(pool id, buffer index)` so it can ride any\n//! queue as an ordinary POD message, and the [`PoolRegistry`]\n//! turns descriptors back into owned guards (see the design\n//! doc's \"Descriptor and registry design (0.7.0)\").\n//!\n//! - [`PoolRegistry::into_desc`] (safe) consumes a guard into\n//!   a descriptor; ownership travels on in the descriptor.\n//! - [`PoolRegistry::resolve`] (unsafe) validates a received\n//!   descriptor and mints the guard back; `unsafe` covers\n//!   only what validation cannot check — ownership\n//!   uniqueness.\n//! - Fixed capacity, no allocation, no unregister: pool ids\n//!   are registry slot indices and never dangle.\n\nuse zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};\n\nuse crate::pool::PoolResolver;\nuse crate::{BufSlot, type_fits};\n\n/// A buffer's travel form: names its pool and buffer index so\n/// any queue can carry it as an ordinary message.\n///\n/// - Plain data on purpose: `FromBytes` means a receiver\n///   mints one from shared bytes anyway, so ownership\n///   discipline lives in [`PoolRegistry::resolve`]'s\n///   contract, not in this type.\n/// - Fields are raw `u32`s (not [`PoolId`]) because the wire\n///   form is untrusted by definition; validation happens at\n///   resolve.\n#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\n#[repr(C)]\npub struct Desc {\n    /// The registry slot of the buffer's pool.\n    pub pool_id: u32,\n    /// The buffer's index within that pool.\n    pub buf_idx: u32,\n}\n\n/// A registered pool's identity within one process's\n/// [`PoolRegistry`] — the value [`register`](PoolRegistry::register)\n/// returns.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct PoolId(u32);\n\nimpl PoolId {\n    /// The raw id (a registry slot index).\n    pub fn as_u32(self) -> u32 {\n        self.0\n    }\n}\n\n/// Errors from [`PoolRegistry`] operations.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RegistryError {\n    /// `register`: the fixed-capacity table is full.\n    Full,\n    /// The pool id names no registered pool.\n    UnknownPoolId,\n    /// `into_desc`: the guard's pool is not the id's entry.\n    WrongPool,\n    /// `resolve`: buffer index out of range for the pool.\n    BadIndex,\n    /// `resolve`: `T`'s geometry does not fit the pool's\n    /// buffers. An `Err`, not the `alloc`-style panic: the\n    /// descriptor selects which pool gets compared, and\n    /// untrusted input must not select a panic.\n    BadType,\n}\n\n/// Per-process table mapping pool ids to this process's\n/// [`PoolResolver`] view of each pool.\n///\n/// - Fixed capacity `N` (const generic): no_std, zero\n///   allocation.\n/// - `register` assigns the next slot index as the pool's id;\n///   there is no unregister, so ids never dangle.\n/// - Registration (`&mut self`) is setup-phase; `into_desc` /\n///   `resolve` take `&self`, so one registry is shared by\n///   reference across threads.\npub struct PoolRegistry<'a, const N: usize> {\n    /// Registered views, dense from slot 0.\n    resolvers: [Option<PoolResolver<'a>>; N],\n    /// Number of registered pools (next id to assign).\n    len: usize,\n}\n\nimpl<'a, const N: usize> PoolRegistry<'a, N> {\n    /// An empty registry.\n    pub const fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            resolvers: [None; N],\n            len: 0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Register a pool's resolver view; returns the assigned\n    /// [`PoolId`] (the next slot index), or\n    /// [`RegistryError::Full`].\n    pub fn register(&mut self, resolver: PoolResolver<'a>) -> Result<PoolId, RegistryError> {\n        if self.len == N {\n            return Err(RegistryError::Full);\n        }\n        let id = self.len as u32;\n        self.resolvers[self.len] = Some(resolver);\n        self.len += 1;\n        Ok(PoolId(id))\n    }\n\n    /// Consume a guard into its descriptor; ownership travels\n    /// on in the [`Desc`] (the usage model's \"in-flight\"\n    /// state).\n    ///\n    /// - O(1): checks the guard's pool identity (header\n    ///   address) against the id's entry, catching id/pool\n    ///   mispairing.\n    /// - The error side hands the guard back, so a miss\n    ///   cannot leak the buffer.\n    pub fn into_desc<T>(\n        &self,\n        pool_id: PoolId,\n        slot: BufSlot<'a, T>,\n    ) -> Result<Desc, (BufSlot<'a, T>, RegistryError)> {\n        let Some(resolver) = self.get(pool_id.0) else {\n            return Err((slot, RegistryError::UnknownPoolId));\n        };\n        if !core::ptr::eq(resolver.header_ptr(), slot.header_ptr()) {\n            return Err((slot, RegistryError::WrongPool));\n        }\n        Ok(Desc {\n            pool_id: pool_id.0,\n            buf_idx: slot.idx(),\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Validate a received descriptor and mint its owned\n    /// guard.\n    ///\n    /// Every validation failure is an `Err`, never a panic:\n    /// unknown pool id, index out of range, `T` geometry\n    /// mismatch (see [`RegistryError::BadType`] for why this\n    /// differs from `alloc`).\n    ///\n    /// # Safety\n    ///\n    /// Validation cannot check ownership; the caller promises:\n    ///\n    /// - `desc` came from [`into_desc`](Self::into_desc) (or\n    ///   an equivalent consumed guard) — it is not invented.\n    /// - It arrived over a channel establishing happens-before\n    ///   with the sender's writes (a ring commit → reserve\n    ///   qualifies).\n    /// - It is resolved exactly once — a second resolve mints\n    ///   a second guard aliasing the same `&mut T`.\n    pub unsafe fn resolve<T>(&self, desc: Desc) -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, RegistryError>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        let Some(resolver) = self.get(desc.pool_id) else {\n            return Err(RegistryError::UnknownPoolId);\n        };\n        if desc.buf_idx >= resolver.buf_count() {\n            return Err(RegistryError::BadIndex);\n        }\n        if !type_fits::<T>(resolver.buf_size()) {\n            return Err(RegistryError::BadType);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: index and T geometry validated above;\n        // ownership uniqueness and ordering are the caller's\n        // contract (this fn's # Safety).\n        Ok(unsafe { resolver.slot_from_idx(desc.buf_idx) })\n    }\n\n    /// Number of registered pools.\n    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {\n        self.len\n    }\n\n    /// True when no pool is registered.\n    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        self.len == 0\n    }\n\n    /// The resolver registered under raw id `id`, if any.\n    fn get(&self, id: u32) -> Option<&PoolResolver<'a>> {\n        self.resolvers.get(id as usize)?.as_ref()\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<const N: usize> Default for PoolRegistry<'_, N> {\n    /// Same as [`PoolRegistry::new`].\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

`-2` is complete and validated — ready for your review. What's in the working copy:

**New API (`src/registry.rs`, +`src/pool.rs` additions, exported from `lib.rs`):**

- `Desc { pool_id: u32, buf_idx: u32 }` — 8-byte POD with the zerocopy derives, so it rides the ring as an ordinary message. Fields are deliberately raw `u32`s and public: the wire form is untrusted by definition.
- `Pool::resolver() -> PoolResolver` — the derived non-allocating view (`Clone + Copy + Send + Sync`, with safety comments justifying each). It holds pub(crate) primitives only; the public surface goes through the registry.
- `PoolRegistry<'a, const N: usize>` — const-generic array, `const fn new()`, `register(resolver) -> PoolId`. `register` is `&mut self` (setup phase); `into_desc`/`resolve` are `&self` so one registry shares across threads by reference.
- `into_desc(pool_id, slot) -> Result<Desc, (BufSlot, RegistryError)>` — safe, O(1) header-identity check, guard handed back on error.
- `unsafe resolve::<T>(desc) -> Result<BufSlot<T>, RegistryError>` — all failures are `Err` (UnknownPoolId / BadIndex / BadType), with the ownership contract in its `# Safety` section exactly as the design doc states it. Uses a new non-panicking `type_fits` alongside `check_type` in `lib.rs`.

**Folded in per your direction:** the Provenance split — its six bullets are now `####` headings (Offsets only / Message provenance / Descriptors / Pool self-description / Pool-id resolution / Trust), the three System-model links retarget to `#pool-id-resolution`, `#descriptors`, `#trust`, and Message provenance gained a "(Deferred as of 0.7.0…)" note. Also fixed a stale pointer in `pool.rs`'s module doc that referenced "todo.md's In Progress block" for the deferral.

Plus the usual: version `0.7.0-2`, ladder entry flipped to done. Validation: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, and all 3 test suites green (18 lib tests; new-code tests are the `-3` commit).

Say the word for the commit description.

> AGENT

Tests are in and the ladder is updated. The six new tests in `src/registry.rs`:

- **`desc_round_trip`** — alloc → write → `into_desc` → `resolve` → read → free, then verifies the freed buffer is genuinely back (all 4 allocate again, 5th is `Exhausted`).
- **`into_desc_checks_pool_identity`** — mispaired id → `WrongPool` *and* the guard comes back usable (the no-leak contract), then succeeds with the right id.
- **`into_desc_unknown_pool_id`** — an id minted by a bigger registry has no entry in a smaller one → `UnknownPoolId`, guard returned.
- **`resolve_rejects_hostile_descs`** — invented descriptors: unknown pool id, out-of-range index, and a `T` too big for the pool's buffers — each the right `Err`, never a panic.
- **`register_full`** — capacity-1 registry rejects the second register.
- **`desc_over_ring_cross_thread`** — the composed protocol end-to-end: 10,000 messages, descriptors carrying pool-buffer ownership through the SPSC ring producer-thread → consumer-thread, 4 buffers recycling under pressure, sequence asserted on every message. This one is the real assurance — it exercises exactly the flow the `-3` demo (and iiac-perf) will run.

fmt clean, clippy `-D warnings` clean, 24/24 tests pass. `-2` now lands API + tests together, and the ladder records why. Ready for the commit description?

> DEVELOPER

Is there a registery per process, If so and how does process X find process Y's registry or maybe there only one in shared memory and all processes use it? Maybe that's in our overview and I missed it?

> AGENT

One registry **per process**, in private memory — and the key insight is that process X never needs to find process Y's registry at all. Here's the model:

The registry itself never crosses a process boundary; only **descriptors** do. When Y sends `Desc { pool_id: 3, buf_idx: 7 }`, X doesn't consult Y's registry — X's *own* registry must have, at slot 3, X's *own* mapping of that same pool. Each process holds a private table whose *values* differ (each process maps the shared pool region at a different local address) but whose *keys* — the pool ids — must agree. The id is the shared vocabulary; the registry is each process's private dictionary for it.

So the real cross-process requirement isn't "find the other registry," it's **id agreement**: every participating process must register the same pool under the same id. How ids get agreed is exactly the open question the design records (`Pool-id allocation`: coordinator-assigned vs derived from the shm object's identity). A registry living in shared memory would be an alternative, but it would defeat itself: the values are per-process pointers, which are meaningless in another process — the whole reason descriptors carry ids instead of pointers.

Your question does expose one honest gap: the current `register()` assigns ids sequentially by registration order, which only produces agreement when a single process assigns all ids (the in-process case we're building). Cross-process, registration order won't match, so the registry will need something like `register_at(id, resolver)` — register under an externally agreed id. That's a small future API addition, not a redesign, but it's worth writing down now.

The overview does say "Each process keeps its own pool registry / it maps pool ids to that process's view (mapping) of each pool" — so it's there, but the id-agreement precondition is implicit. Since `-2` isn't committed yet, let me make it explicit:

> AGENT

Both spots updated: the overview's registry bullet now has two new sub-bullets (registries private, only descriptors cross; ids are the shared vocabulary with a link to the open question), and the design section's `PoolRegistry` bullet records the sequential-assignment limitation and the future `register_at` shape.

Does that settle it, or do you want to dig further into the id-agreement schemes before we commit `-2`?

> DEVELOPER

It's going to be an interesting problem. And this points out a problem in line 378 you point to open questions but which open question, I think open questions should be markdown sections so it is locatable. Thougts, no actions.

> AGENT

Agreed on both counts — it's the same disease the Provenance split cured, and Open questions is if anything a *stronger* case for the treatment. Two thoughts:

**Why Open questions especially deserves headings.** Each open question is a natural unit of external reference — todo entries, chores sections, commit bodies, and now the System model all want to cite *a specific question*, not the pile. My `[open question](#open-questions)` link on line 378 is exactly the failure mode: the reader lands at the section top and has to hunt for "Pool-id allocation." So the trigger rule generalizes slightly: promote not just when two links want different spots, but when any link wants a target finer than what exists. `#### Pool-id allocation`, `#### Setup plane`, `#### Message header shape`.

**A pleasant property of the resolution lifecycle.** Open questions eventually migrate to `## Resolved questions`, which sounds like anchor churn — but GitHub anchors are derived from the heading *text*, not its position. Moving `#### Pool-id allocation` from Open to Resolved keeps `#pool-id-allocation` working in every inbound link, unchanged. So headings actually make the migration *safer* than bullets: today, resolving a question rewrites a bullet and silently strands any prose that described where to find it; with headings the link follows the question wherever it lives. The only rename hazard is retitling a question, which the pre-commit dangling-anchor check catches.

The one counterweight is the same as before — heading proliferation making the doc read like an index — but three questions with a paragraph each is well past the threshold where that concern loses; these are already reference targets in practice.

When you give the word I'd do the split and retarget line 378 to `#pool-id-allocation` — folded into `-2` like the Provenance split, since it's the same link-hygiene work and `-2` is still open.

660	  (registry slot index, no unregister); cross-process
661	  remains open: coordinator-assigned versus derived from

663	- **Setup plane** — how a process discovers and maps a pool it
664	  hasn't seen: pre-arranged at init (embedded-friendly,
665	  allocation-free after startup) versus fd-passing over a
666	  Unix socket (Linux-friendly, dynamic); likely both, as
667	  profiles.
668	- **Message header shape** — 0.7.0 settled the near half:
669	  provenance travels in the descriptor, a type tag is
670	  payload-level, and the in-buffer header is deferred (see
671	  [Descriptor and registry design](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070)).
672	  Still open: whether a length (or anything else) joins a
673	  future in-buffer header, and its layout against the
674	  embedded next-link — the link is load-bearing for three
675	  states (free-stack, pending FIFO, future lists), so they
676	  must be laid out together when that header lands.
677	
678	## Resolved questions
679	
680	- **Cross-process trust** — resolved in 0.3.0-4: the
681	  all-atomic header plus the geometry snapshot mean a hostile
682	  peer cannot cause UB, only garbage messages (still valid
683	  `T`s — zerocopy guarantees representation, not sense) or
684	  spurious Full/Empty. `attach` remains `unsafe` for the
685	  mapping-liveness contract, not for peer trust.
686	- **`split()` once-guard** — direction chosen: an
687	  endpoint-claims word in the header (CAS to claim the
688	  producer / consumer role, second claimant gets an error),

  "old_string": "### Open questions\n\n- **Pool-id allocation** — in-process settled in 0.7.0\n  (registry slot index, no unregister); cross-process\n  remains open: coordinator-assigned versus derived from\n  the shm object's identity.\n- **Setup plane** — how a process discovers and maps a pool it\n  hasn't seen: pre-arranged at init (embedded-friendly,\n  allocation-free after startup) versus fd-passing over a\n  Unix socket (Linux-friendly, dynamic); likely both, as\n  profiles.\n- **Message header shape** — 0.7.0 settled the near half:\n  provenance travels in the descriptor, a type tag is\n  payload-level, and the in-buffer header is deferred (see\n  [Descriptor and registry design](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070)).\n  Still open: whether a length (or anything else) joins a\n  future in-buffer header, and its layout against the\n  embedded next-link — the link is load-bearing for three\n  states (free-stack, pending FIFO, future lists), so they\n  must be laid out together when that header lands.",
  "new_string": "### Open questions\n\nOne question per heading, each directly linkable; a\nresolved question migrates to\n[Resolved questions](#resolved-questions), keeping its\nheading (and so its anchor).\n\n#### Pool-id allocation\n\nIn-process settled in 0.7.0 (registry slot index, no\nunregister); cross-process remains open:\ncoordinator-assigned versus derived from the shm object's\nidentity.\n\n#### Setup plane\n\nHow a process discovers and maps a pool it hasn't seen:\npre-arranged at init (embedded-friendly, allocation-free\nafter startup) versus fd-passing over a Unix socket\n(Linux-friendly, dynamic); likely both, as profiles.\n\n#### Message header shape\n\n0.7.0 settled the near half: provenance travels in the\ndescriptor, a type tag is payload-level, and the in-buffer\nheader is deferred (see\n[Descriptor and registry design](#descriptor-and-registry-design-070)).\nStill open: whether a length (or anything else) joins a\nfuture in-buffer header, and its layout against the\nembedded next-link — the link is load-bearing for three\nstates (free-stack, pending FIFO, future lists), so they\nmust be laid out together when that header lands."

**Proposed commit description:**

```
feat: pool registry + descriptor resolve

The descriptor layer's in-process slice: an 8-byte Desc
rides any queue as a POD message, a per-process
PoolRegistry turns guards into descriptors and back, with
protocol tests folded in so the API never lands untested.

- src/registry.rs (new)
  - Desc { pool_id, buf_idx }: POD wire form, raw u32
    fields by design
  - PoolId, RegistryError, PoolRegistry<N>: const-generic
    table, register -> PoolId, safe O(1) into_desc
    returning the guard on error, unsafe resolve with
    all-Err validation + ownership # Safety contract
  - tests: round-trip, pool-identity + unknown-id
    into_desc, hostile-desc resolve, registry full, 10k
    descs over the ring cross-thread with recycling
- src/pool.rs
  - PoolResolver: non-allocating Send+Sync view;
    Pool::resolver() derives it from the handle (no second
    region borrow); pub(crate) slot_from_idx mints guards
  - BufSlot: pub(crate) header_ptr/idx accessors
  - module doc: stale todo.md pointer -> design doc
- src/lib.rs: mod registry + exports; type_fits
  (non-panicking check_type for resolve)
- notes/ring-buffer-design.md
  - Provenance and descriptors: bullets promoted to ####
    headings (directly linkable); overview links retarget
  - Open questions: one #### per question; anchor survives
    resolution migration; links retarget
  - registry id-agreement notes: private registries, ids
    are the shared vocabulary, future register_at
- notes/todo.md: ladder -2 done (tests folded in), demo
  renumbered to -3
- Cargo.toml: version 0.7.0-2
- Cargo.lock: version
```

Approve and I'll `vc-x1 push main`.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,\n/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std\n/// channel); return elapsed seconds.",
  "new_string": "/// The composed flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: alloc a\n/// pool message, send its descriptor through the ring,\n/// resolve and free it; return elapsed seconds.\nfn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t() -> f64 {\n    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers\n    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();\n    let pool_id = registry\n        .register(pool.resolver())\n        .unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut buf = pool\n                    .alloc::<Msg>()\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: alloc+free per iteration, DEPTH never exceeded\n                buf.seq = i;\n                let desc = registry\n                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf)\n                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register\n                match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {\n                    Ok(mut slot) => {\n                        *slot = desc;\n                        slot.commit();\n                    }\n                    Err(Full) => {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n                    }\n                }\n                match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {\n                    Ok(slot) => {\n                        let desc = *slot;\n                        slot.release();\n                        // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into\n                        // the ring by into_desc above and is\n                        // resolved exactly once, same thread.\n                        let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }\n                            .unwrap(); // OK: desc came from into_desc on this pool\n                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                        msg.free();\n                    }\n                    Err(Empty) => {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\");\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// The composed flow producer-thread → consumer-thread:\n/// alloc + fill pool messages on the producer, descriptors\n/// cross the SPSC ring, the consumer resolves and frees;\n/// return elapsed seconds.\n///\n/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and\n///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place\n///   them (the number then depends on where they land).\nfn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {\n    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers\n    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();\n    let pool_id = registry\n        .register(pool.resolver())\n        .unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room\n    let registry = &registry;\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(p);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut buf = loop {\n                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {\n                        Ok(buf) => break buf,\n                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                    }\n                };\n                buf.seq = i;\n                let desc = registry\n                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf)\n                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register\n                loop {\n                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {\n                        Ok(mut slot) => {\n                            *slot = desc;\n                            slot.commit();\n                            break;\n                        }\n                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(c);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let desc = loop {\n                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {\n                        Ok(slot) => {\n                            let desc = *slot;\n                            slot.release();\n                            break desc;\n                        }\n                        Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),\n                    }\n                };\n                // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into the ring\n                // by the producer and read after the commit →\n                // reserve handoff (happens-before); each is\n                // resolved exactly once.\n                let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: descs here only come from the producer's into_desc\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.free();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// Alloc + fill COUNT messages on an allocator thread,\n/// free them on a freer thread (guards cross a std\n/// channel); return elapsed seconds."
}
```

What the numbers say (single runs, usual caveat): the composed 1t at 11.8 ns is almost exactly ring (2.7) + pool alloc/free (9.8) — the descriptor layer itself adds nearly nothing. Same-core composed beats the std-channel version (44.5 vs 59.4 ns), the apples-to-apples line iiac-perf will want. Diff-cores composed (317.7) came in *above* std_mpsc (284.8) this run — plausibly real (the composed flow ping-pongs ring lines *and* free-stack *and* buffer lines across L3 domains) but within the swing we've seen between runs; the mean/stdev work would settle it.

The changes: two new demo functions (`spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t` pinned to core 0, `_2t` with the full pin ladder) running alloc → `into_desc` → `Desc` through the ring → `resolve` → assert → free; module doc updated (composed form "runs between them" instead of "is the next cycle"); the stale footer line removed; overview gained the "as built, everything is single-process" sub-bullet you asked for; version `0.7.0-3`, ladder marked done. Validation: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, 24/24 tests, install (binary reports `0.7.0-3`).

Ready for the commit description?

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Alloc → write → free COUNT messages on one thread pinned\n/// to cpu 0 — the pool's own cost, no channel, no second\n/// thread; return elapsed seconds.",
  "new_string": "/// The std-channel flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: alloc\n/// a pool message, move its guard through a sync_channel,\n/// receive and free it; return elapsed seconds.\nfn std_mpsc_one_pool_msg_1t() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers\n    let (tx, rx) = std::sync::mpsc::sync_channel::<BufSlot<'_, Msg>>(DEPTH as usize);\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut buf = pool\n                    .alloc::<Msg>()\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: alloc+free per iteration, DEPTH never exceeded\n                buf.seq = i;\n                tx.send(buf).unwrap(); // OK: capacity DEPTH, at most one in flight\n                let buf = rx.recv().unwrap(); // OK: just sent on this thread\n                assert_eq!(buf.seq, i);\n                buf.free();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// Alloc → write → free COUNT messages on one thread pinned\n/// to cpu 0 — the pool's own cost, no channel, no second\n/// thread; return elapsed seconds."
}

> DEVELOPER

We should be able to get a desc for a pool outside the loop and just populate the buf and I suggest renaming buf to buf_slot?

> AGENT

Two parts to this — the rename is straightforward, but "get a desc outside the loop" runs into the ownership design in an instructive way. Let me explain what I'll do:

The guard (`BufSlot`) and the descriptor are the *two exclusive forms of ownership* — `into_desc` consumes the guard because holding both would let the sender write while the receiver reads. So "populate the buf" and "already have the desc" can't coexist; the conversion *is* the send-side handoff. What we **can** hoist in the 1t flow is the alloc/free: alloc once outside the timed loop, and inside just populate → `into_desc` → ring → `resolve` → assert, with the resolved guard carrying into the next iteration and one `free()` after the loop. That isolates pure messaging cost (conversion + ring transfer) from the pool cycle. The 2t flow can't hoist at all: ownership genuinely leaves the producer each message, so the producer's next buffer must come from the free-stack — alloc/free per message *is* that flow, not overhead. 258	/// The composed flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: alloc a
259	/// pool message, send its descriptor through the ring,
260	/// resolve and free it; return elapsed seconds.
261	fn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t() -> f64 {
262	    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
263	    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
264	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
265	    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();
266	    let pool_id = registry.register(pool.resolver()).unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room
267	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
268	        Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
269	            .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
270	            .split();
271	
272	    let start = Instant::now();
273	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
274	        s.spawn(move || {
275	            pin_to_cpu(0);
276	            for i in 0..COUNT {
277	                let mut buf = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // OK: alloc+free per iteration, DEPTH never exceeded
278	                buf.seq = i;
279	                let desc = registry
280	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf)
281	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
282	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
283	                match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
284	                    Ok(mut slot) => {
285	                        *slot = desc;
286	                        slot.commit();
287	                    }
288	                    Err(Full) => {
289	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
290	                    }
291	                }
292	                match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
293	                    Ok(slot) => {
294	                        let desc = *slot;
295	                        slot.release();
296	                        // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into
297	                        // the ring by into_desc above and is
298	                        // resolved exactly once, same thread.
299	                        let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: desc came from into_desc on this pool
300	                        assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
301	                        msg.free();
302	                    }
303	                    Err(Empty) => {
304	                        panic!("spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN");
305	                    }
306	                }
307	            }
308	        });
309	    });

311	}

> AGENT

Both changes are in, validated, and the hoist paid off visibly:

- **`spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_1t`** now allocs one message before the timed loop; each iteration populates it, `into_desc` → ring → `resolve` brings the guard back for the next iteration, and the single `free()` lands after the loop. The per-iteration conversion stays — the guard and descriptor are the two exclusive ownership forms, so converting *is* the handoff and can't hoist. The doc comment records both that and why the 2t variant keeps alloc/free per message (ownership genuinely leaves the producer there). Effect: 1t composed dropped from ~11.9 to **7.7 ns/msg** — pure messaging cost (ring 2.6 + conversion/resolve ~5), with the pool cycle now cleanly separated into `pool_alloc_free_1t`'s line.
- **`buf` → `buf_slot`** on every `BufSlot`-typed local (composed 2t, both std_mpsc functions, `pool_alloc_free_1t`). I left `global_alloc_free_1t`'s local as `buf` since it's a `Box<Msg>`, not a `BufSlot` — renaming it would claim a type it isn't.

> AGENT

Agreed — and the fix is a layer, not sugar. Thoughts:

**Target shape.** What the demo loop wants to be:

```rust
let mut msg = tx.loan_spin::<Msg>();   // alloc from the sender's pool
msg.seq = i;
tx.send_spin(msg);                     // into_desc + reserve + write + commit
```
```rust
let msg = rx.recv_spin::<Msg>();       // reserve + read desc + release + resolve
assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
msg.free();
```

**The mechanism: paired endpoints, not helper functions.** A `DescSender` owning `(Producer, PoolId, &PoolRegistry — or the Pool itself)` and a `DescReceiver` owning `(Consumer, &PoolRegistry)`. The composite operations collapse the ceremony, but the deep win is safety, not line count: today every call site carries the `unsafe resolve` and its exactly-once contract. If descriptors only enter the ring through the paired sender's `send` — which consumed a guard to produce each one — then the receiver's `recv` satisfies resolve's contract *by construction*, and `recv` can be a **safe** function with the `unsafe` audited once, inside the crate, instead of at every user call site. The `map_err(|(_, e)| e)` noise disappears too, because users never touch a `Desc` at all — it becomes an implementation detail of the pair.

**Blocking stays layered.** The primitives stay non-blocking (`send` → `Err((msg, Full))` handing the guard back so nothing leaks, `recv` → `Err(Empty)`, `loan` → `Err(Exhausted)`), preserving the embedded floor; `_spin` variants are three-line conveniences on top. The futex/eventfd blocking layer from Ideas would later wrap these same endpoints.

**Owning alloc on the sender is the natural home.** `tx.loan()` — iceoryx2's loan/send shape exactly, which the design doc already told us to steal from. It also carries the single-popper token naturally: the sender is `&mut`-exclusive, so "one owning allocator" becomes structural rather than documented.

**One design question to settle when we do it:** does the sender own the `Pool` (simple, but locks one pool per sender) or take `(PoolId, BufSlot)` per send (any registered pool over one queue — the "any message over any queue" promise)? Probably: own a default pool for `loan`, but accept any guard in `send` via the registry.

**Where it goes:** not this commit — `-3` is the demo, and this is a real API cycle ("feat: descriptor queue endpoints", or `loan`/`send`/`recv` naming to taste). I'd add it as a `## Todo` entry at rank #1 when we close 0.7.0 — arguably it should even precede the iiac-perf work, since iiac-perf would otherwise inline all this ceremony too.

Want me to add the todo entry as part of `-3`'s notes changes, or hold everything for the close-out?

> DEVELOPER

I wonder we make an intermediate later where what to do on Empty/Full is a closure, so something like: `buf_slot =  pool.get_slot<Msg>(||spin_loop)` thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

I wonder we we might use a closure on Empty/Full,  something like: `buf_slot =  pool.get_slot<Msg>(||spin_loop)` thoughts?

> DEVELOPER

I wonder if we might use a closure for Empty/Full handling,  something like: `buf_slot =  pool.get_slot<Msg>(||spin_loop)` thoughts?

> AGENT

Yes — that's the right generalization, with one refinement. Thoughts:

**A closure turns the wait loop into an injected policy.** `_spin` variants hard-code one policy; a closure parameter makes the operation generic over *how to wait* — spin, `yield_now`, sleep-with-backoff, whatever — so we don't grow a method per policy per operation. The demo's nine-line loop becomes exactly your sketch, and `_spin` (if we keep it at all) is just the closure pre-filled.

**The refinement: let the closure vote on giving up.** A `FnMut()` that can only spin loops forever — fine for a demo, wrong for real callers who need timeouts. Make it `FnMut(u32) -> bool` (attempt count in, "keep waiting" out):

```rust
pub fn alloc_with<T>(&mut self, mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool)
    -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, Exhausted>
```

Then the whole policy space falls out of one signature: pure spin `|_| { spin_loop(); true }`, bounded `|n| n < 10_000`, progressive backoff `|n| { if n < 100 { spin_loop() } else { yield_now() }; true }`, and a deadline via a captured `Instant`. `Err` comes back only when the policy quits, so the non-blocking contract survives — the primitive `alloc`/`reserve_slot` stay exactly as they are, and `_with` is a layer over them. A few canned policies as plain `fn`s (`policy::spin`, `policy::spin_then_yield(n)`) would cover the common cases without any trait machinery.

**It applies uniformly** to all three wait points — `alloc`/`Exhausted`, producer `reserve_slot`/`Full`, consumer `reserve_slot`/`Empty` — and composes perfectly with the endpoint layer from the last discussion: `loan_with`, `send_with`, `recv_with` all take the same policy shape. That uniformity is the argument for doing it as one design rather than a `get_slot` one-off on `Pool`.

**One honest limitation to record:** a closure policy covers *polling* strategies only. True blocking (futex, eventfd, async wakers) needs the other endpoint to issue a wake — a protocol over the header's user line, not something a wait closure can do alone. So this doesn't replace the blocking-layer Idea; it's the tier between "raw non-blocking" and "real blocking," and probably eliminates 90% of the need for the latter.

Both this and the endpoints feel like one coherent follow-up cycle: endpoints give the 3-line shape, wait policies give the loop-free waits. I'd write them as a single `## Todo` entry ("descriptor queue endpoints + wait policies") at close-out. Sound right?

> DEVELOPER

So we have more than one "closure" Type?

> AGENT

Depends which sense of "type" — the answer is "one signature, many machine types, our choice of how to accept them":

**One signature.** All three wait points (alloc/`Exhausted`, producer/`Full`, consumer/`Empty`) take the same shape: `FnMut(u32) -> bool`. The policy never needs to know *what* it's waiting for — the operation retries its own condition; the closure just decides whether/how to keep waiting. So conceptually there's exactly one "wait policy" contract in the design.

**Many concrete types.** In Rust every closure *expression* has its own unique, unnameable type — even two textually identical `|n| n < 100`s are distinct types. `FnMut(u32) -> bool` is not a type; it's a trait bound that all of them satisfy.

**Three ways the API can accept that**, and this is the real design choice:

- `impl FnMut(u32) -> bool` (generic) — monomorphized: each distinct closure stamps out its own specialized copy of `alloc_with`, fully inlined, zero overhead. The spin policy compiles to literally the loop we write by hand today. Cost: one copy of the function per distinct policy in the binary.
- `&mut dyn FnMut(u32) -> bool` (trait object) — one shared copy of the function, closure called through an indirect call. A hair slower per retry, but retries are the slow path anyway. This is the code-size-friendly option if an embedded target ever cares.
- `fn(u32) -> bool` (plain function pointer) — no captures allowed, so no deadline-carrying policies, but the canned ones (`policy::spin`) are just ordinary `fn`s and coerce to this.

I'd take `impl FnMut(u32) -> bool` in the public API — it's the idiomatic zero-cost choice, and it accepts all three kinds above (plain `fn`s and capturing closures alike). If binary bloat ever shows up on an embedded build, an internal `dyn` core with a thin generic wrapper gets one copy without changing the API. So: one policy concept, one bound, and monomorphization details stay invisible to callers.

> AGENT

That's the right structure — a composition ladder rather than one blessed signature. Concretely three tiers:

1. **Primitive (exists, unchanged):** `alloc` / `reserve_slot` returning `Err` immediately. The only tier with protocol knowledge; everything above is compos

> DEVELOPER

I was thinking multiple signatures, in the end users can compose to whatever they need, the simplest is just their own "get<Msg>()" and no closure, but we should supply common ones we use as a model.

> AGENT

Right — that reframes what the shipped helpers are *for*: not coverage of the policy space, but worked examples proving the composition works. The tiers:

1. **Primitive (exists, unchanged):** `alloc` / `reserve_slot` → immediate `Err(Exhausted / Full / Empty)`. The only tier that knows the protocol; everything above is composition, so nothing above can break an invariant.
2. **The hook:** `alloc_with(policy)` / endpoint `loan_with` / `send_with` / `recv_with` — the one seam where a wait policy plugs in.
3. **Shipped models:** the handful we ourselves use in the demo and tests — probably just `_spin` (`= _with(|_| { spin_loop(); true })`) and maybe a bounded variant. Each is a two-line wrapper whose body *is* the documentation: "here's how you build yours."
4. **User compositions:** their own `get::<Msg>()` — no closure in *their* signature because the policy is baked into their wrapper, exactly as domain code wants it. An app writes `fn get(&mut self) -> Msg` once, with its own timeout/backoff/panic-on-starvation choice, and the rest of the app never sees a wait loop or a closure again.

The discipline that keeps it honest: the crate ships mechanisms plus only the policies it dogfoods — anything we ship must be used by our own demo/tests, or it's speculative API. And tier 4 costing the user two lines is the proof the seam is right; if their `get` needs more than that, the hook is wrong.

This all folds into the close-out todo entry — "descriptor queue endpoints + wait-policy hook, shipped policies as models" — and the demo itself becomes the first tier-4 consumer when that cycle lands. For *this* commit I'd leave the demo's explicit loops as-is; they're the honest picture of today's API, and their verbosity is now a recorded motivation for the next cycle. Still holding for your word on the `-3` description.

> DEVELOPER

And in iiac-perf or a variant specifically for zc-ring-x1, the performance can be measured.

> AGENT

Exactly — that's the missing piece that makes the whole layering defensible. The demo stays an eyeball smoke-baseline (single runs, no stats — as you flagged earlier), and iiac-perf's calibrated harness answers the questions the demo can't:

- **Is the abstraction actually free?** The claim that `send`/`recv` endpoints and a monomorphized wait closure compile to the same code as today's hand-rolled loops is verifiable: benchmark the tier-3 wrapper against the inline loop — the delta should be zero within noise. If it isn't, the seam is wrong and we find out *before* users build on it.
- **The real comparison with statistics:** composed descriptor flow vs `std::mpsc` vs crossbeam etc., mean/stdev/p99 across placements — the `zcring-1t`/`zcring-2t` mirrors of `mpsc_1t`/`mpsc_2t` the Ideas entry already sketches, now with the composed `zcring-pool-2t` joining them. The tail-latency claim in the Ideas notes (pool beats malloc on p99 because no arena locks, no brk/mmap) is also only answerable there.
- **Policy costs:** spin vs spin-then-yield vs bounded — measurable per policy since each is just a closure handed to the same hook.

Whether it lives in iiac-perf proper or a zc-ring-specific variant can be decided when we get there — iiac-perf proper seems right since cross-library comparison is its whole point, and the endpoints will make the harness code as short there as in the demo.

So the sequencing after 0.7.0 closes: endpoints + wait-policy cycle first (gives the 3-line API), then the iiac-perf integration measures it. I'll capture both in the close-out's todo entries. Ready to show the `-3` commit description whenever you are.

Noted — that ordering is smart: the wait-policy hook + `_spin` + the three-way iiac-perf comparison (raw loop vs `_with(closure)` vs `_spin`) *validates the seam empirically before* the endpoints cycle builds on it. ## feat: message pool allocator

Commits: [[15]],[[16]],[[17]],[[18]],[[19]],[[20]]

Todo #1 (decouple "get a message" from "send it"): the ring's
reserve_slot fuses allocation, queue position, and a
publication window, so getting a buffer implied sending it
now. The pool is the allocation half of the messaging layer —
simplicity-first, to hone the API and give iiac-perf a
baseline.

- Region shape mirrors the ring: all-atomic two-line
  PoolHeader (cold geometry / contended `first_free_idx`),
  magic "ZCP1", magic-last init/attach handshake, geometry
  snapshots, hostile-peer posture (validated pops fail toward
  Exhausted, never out of bounds).
- Intrusive LIFO free-stack with zero per-buffer overhead: a
  free buffer's first word is its next-link; allocated
  buffers carry no header. Pool-id provenance deferred to the
  descriptor-queue cycle (pool layout_version bump).
- Ownership decoupling: `BufSlot` borrows the region
  lifetime, not the pool handle — many live guards at once;
  `alloc` takes `&mut self` as the single-popper token (no
  ABA); `free(self)` is the MPSC push, callable wherever the
  guard moved; drop-without-free leaks (RAII alternative
  recorded in Ideas).
- Usage model written before the tests (README: roles, buffer
  lifecycle, what "send" means pre-descriptor-queues) and the
  tests pin it: 11 pool tests incl. cross-magic attach
  rejection, corruption-repair, type edges (mixed sizes,
  align(32), too-big panics, ZST), threaded allocator/freer
  split; all under Miri.
- Naming honed in review: `cache_line_size` (verb-free),
  `first_free_idx`, `next_buf_idx`, `CACHE_LINE_SIZE` /
  `TEST_CACHE_LINE_SIZE`.

140	
141	## Usage model: roles and buffer lifecycle
142	
143	Who may do what, per object and per buffer state. The tests
144	exercise exactly what this model permits; anything outside it
145	is a contract violation even where the compiler cannot reject
146	it.
147	
148	**Roles.** Roles are per *object*, and one thread may hold
149	several roles across objects (a typical sender is one pool's
150	allocator and one ring's producer):
151	
152	- **Ring** — exactly one producer and one consumer, ever
153	  (SPSC): in-process, `split()` hands out each endpoint
154	  once; cross-process, one producing and one consuming
155	  process by contract. An endpoint reserves at most one
156	  slot at a time (guard holds the endpoint borrow).
157	- **Pool** — exactly one *allocator* at a time (the
158	  single-popper contract on `Pool::attach`; in-process,
159	  `alloc` takes `&mut self`), and **any number of freers**:
160	  whoever holds a `BufSlot` may free it, from any thread or
161	  process attached to the pool.
162	
163	**Buffer lifecycle.** A pool buffer is always in exactly one
164	state, with one party allowed to touch it:
165	
166	- **free** — on the free-stack; the pool protocol owns it.
167	  Nobody reads or writes it except through alloc/free
168	  machinery (its first word is the next-link).
169	- **allocated** — popped by `alloc`, owned by the `BufSlot`
170	  holder: exclusive read/write through the guard, held as
171	  long as desired, moved between threads freely (`Send`).
172	  No other party — including the pool — may touch the
173	  bytes.
174	- **in-flight** — *placeholder, vacant this cycle*: after a
175	  send over a descriptor queue the sender must no longer
176	  touch the buffer and the receiver owns it on receipt.
177	  Until descriptor queues exist there is no in-flight
178	  state.
179	- **freed** — `BufSlot::free` pushes it back; ownership
180	  returns to the pool protocol the instant the CAS lands.
181	  Use-after-free of the guard is unrepresentable (`free`
182	  consumes it); dropping without `free` leaks the buffer.
183	
184	**What "send" means this cycle.** Transferring a message =
185	moving its `BufSlot` (an ordinary Rust move; `Send` lets it
186	cross threads). The ring and the pool do not yet compose:
187	a `BufSlot` cannot travel through a ring slot, and no API
188	reconstructs buffer access from a bare index — both arrive
189	with the descriptor-queue cycle. Ring messages this cycle
190	are by-value `T`s in slots, as before.
191	
192	**Where this is heading.** The ring alone is a building
193	block; the payoff is the two composed — the ring carrying
194	tiny descriptors while payloads stay put in pool buffers.
195	Illustrative only (this API does not exist yet; names will
196	change):
197	
198	```rust,ignore
199	// Sender: pool allocator + ring producer.
200	let mut msg = pool.alloc::<Msg>()?; // get a message
201	msg.val = 42;                       // fill it in place
202	producer.send(msg)?;                // consumes the BufSlot;
203	                                    // only its index travels
204	                                    // through the ring
205	
206	// Receiver: ring consumer + freer (other thread or process).
207	let desc = consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>()?;
208	let msg = pool.resolve::<Msg>(&desc)?; // zero-copy view
209	//                  ... read msg ...
210	msg.free();     // buffer back to its pool
211	desc.release(); // ring slot free for the next descriptor
212	```
213	
214	One allocation's bytes are written once and never copied —
215	not by send, not by receive, not across the process
216	boundary.
217	
218	**Trust.** Unchanged from the ring: every word in shared
219	memory is untrusted input. The pool validates the head and
220	next-link at every pop and fails toward `Exhausted`; a
221	hostile peer can degrade service (garbage messages, lost
222	buffers, spurious exhaustion), never cause UB on this side.
223	
224	## Testing
225	
226	- `cargo test` — the full suite, including a threaded stress
227	  test and a u32-index-wrap test.
228	- `cargo run --example readme` — the Overview example above
229	  (committed as [examples/readme.rs](examples/readme.rs) so
230	  `cargo clippy --all-targets` keeps it compiling against the
231	  real API).
232	- `cargo run --example pool_readme` — the Message pool
233	  example above
234	  ([examples/pool_readme.rs](examples/pool_readme.rs), same
235	  convention).
236	- `cargo run --release` — the demo binary
237	  ([src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs](src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs)):
238	  a four-line throughput scoreboard (msgs/sec and ns/msg) —
239	  the ring moving typed messages in place across threads,
240	  the pool's allocator/freer role split (guards crossing a
241	  std channel), the pool's raw single-thread alloc/free,
242	  and the same loop through the global allocator for
243	  comparison. Eyeball numbers, not a benchmark (calibrated
244	  measurement lives in iiac-perf). Installable:
245	  `cargo install --path . --locked`, then `zc-ring-x1-demo`;
246	  `-V` prints the version-of-record so you know which build
247	  you are testing. An example run:
248	
249	  ```text
250	  $ zc-ring-x1-demo
251	  zc-ring-x1 0.6.2
252	  demo: 1,000,000 messages each, depth 64
253	  ring (SPSC, in-place):             18,723,550 msgs/sec     53.4 ns/msg
254	  pool (alloc here -> free there):    2,993,315 msgs/sec    334.1 ns/msg
255	  pool (alloc/free, 1 thread):      100,962,781 msgs/sec      9.9 ns/msg
256	  global (Box::new/drop, 1 thread):  107,037,570 msgs/sec      9.3 ns/msg
257	  (composed descriptor flow arrives with the descriptor-queue cycle)
258	  ```
259	- `cargo +nightly miri test` — the full suite under
260	  [Miri](https://github.com/rust-lang/miri), which checks the
261	  `unsafe` code against the memory model; it has caught two
262	  real Stacked Borrows bugs here (an init retag in 0.3.0-4, a
263	  guard aliasing race in 0.3.0-6 — the latter only visible
264	  with the threaded test included). One-time setup:
265	  `rustup component add --toolchain nightly miri`. The
266	  threaded stress test runs a reduced message count under
267	  Miri (interpreted spin loops are slow).
268	
269	## Releasing
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/notes/chores/chores-01.md",
  "old_string": "## feat: descriptor queues over the SPSC ring\n\nCommits:\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "## feat: descriptor queues over the SPSC ring\n\nCommits: [[24]],[[25]],[[26]],[[27]]\n\nGetting a pool buffer must not imply sending it, and the\nring alone fuses allocation, queue position, and\npublication. Descriptors — small `(pool id, buffer index)`\nvalues sent through the existing SPSC ring — separate them,\nso any pool message travels over any queue. This cycle\nbuilt the in-process slice: the descriptor type, pool ids\nvia a per-process registry, and guard ↔ descriptor\nconversion; cross-process setup (mapping exchange, pool-id\ncoordination) stays design\n[details](../ring-buffer-design.md#descriptor-and-registry-design-070).\n\n- The guard and the descriptor are the two exclusive forms\n  of buffer ownership; converting is the send-side handoff.\n  `into_desc` is safe (guard handed back on error, no leak\n  path); `resolve` is the one `unsafe` — validation is\n  all-`Err` (a hostile descriptor selects which pool gets\n  compared, so it must not select a panic), and `unsafe`\n  covers only what validation cannot check: ownership\n  uniqueness plus happens-before delivery.\n- `PoolResolver` is a view derived from the pool handle —\n  no second region borrow, dodging the Stacked Borrows\n  retag hazard init documents. Registries are per-process\n  and private; only descriptors cross boundaries, and pool\n  ids are the shared vocabulary (cross-process\n  `register_at` under an agreed id is recorded against the\n  pool-id-allocation open question).\n- Protocol tests folded into the feat commit rather than a\n  separate test step, so the API never landed untested;\n  the composed cross-thread test (descs over the ring,\n  buffers recycling) is the demo/iiac-perf flow in\n  miniature. Full suite clean under Miri.\n- Demo grew the composed flow with the same placement\n  ladder as the raw ring, output regrouped so like\n  compares with like; the 1t variant allocates outside the\n  timed loop, isolating messaging cost (~8 ns/msg vs the\n  std-channel counterpart's ~35) from the pool cycle.\n- Design doc gained the System model overview (rules at a\n  glance, every bullet linking to its expanding section)\n  and a linkability convention: sub-topics wanted by\n  inbound links become `####` headings (Provenance, Open\n  questions), whose anchors follow their titles even when\n  a section migrates.\n\n### As-built ladder\n\n- 0.7.0-0 chore: open descriptor queue cycle\n- 0.7.0-1 docs: settle descriptor design questions\n- 0.7.0-2 feat: pool registry + descriptor resolve\n- 0.7.0-3 feat: demo descriptor flow ring + pool\n- 0.7.0 feat: descriptor queues over the SPSC ring\n  (close-out)\n\n### Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies\n\nDesign captured from the post-demo review; promoted to\n`## Todo` #1 and #2.\n\n- The demo's send path is ~20 lines of ceremony. Target\n  shape is ~3: `loan` → populate → `send`. Paired\n  `DescSender` (ring producer + pool + registry access;\n  `loan` carries the single-popper token structurally) and\n  `DescReceiver` (consumer + registry). The deep win is\n  safety, not line count: descriptors entering the ring\n  only through the paired sender make `recv` satisfy\n  resolve's contract by construction — the `unsafe` gets\n  audited once inside the crate instead of at every call\n  site. iceoryx2's loan/send shape, as the design doc's\n  prior-art note anticipated.\n- Wait policies as an injected closure: `_with(impl\n  FnMut(u32) -> bool)` — attempt count in, keep-waiting\n  out — uniformly over the three wait points (Exhausted /\n  Full / Empty). Tiers: non-blocking primitive (unchanged,\n  the only tier with protocol knowledge), the `_with`\n  hook, shipped policies we dogfood (`_spin`) as the model\n  users copy, and user compositions (their own\n  `get::<Msg>()`, policy baked in, no closure in their\n  signature). Polling only — true blocking (futex,\n  eventfd) needs a peer wake over the user line and stays\n  a separate layer.\n- Validation order: the wait-policy hook lands first and\n  iiac-perf measures raw loop vs closure vs `_spin` — the\n  seam must be zero-cost before the endpoints build on it.\n\n# References"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/README.md",
  "old_string": "**What \"send\" means this cycle.** Transferring a message =\nmoving its `BufSlot` (an ordinary Rust move; `Send` lets it\ncross threads). The ring and the pool do not yet compose:\na `BufSlot` cannot travel through a ring slot, and no API\nreconstructs buffer access from a bare index — both arrive\nwith the descriptor-queue cycle. Ring messages this cycle\nare by-value `T`s in slots, as before.\n\n**Where this is heading.** The ring alone is a building\nblock; the payoff is the two composed — the ring carrying\ntiny descriptors while payloads stay put in pool buffers.\nIllustrative only (this API does not exist yet; names will\nchange):\n\n```rust,ignore\n// Sender: pool allocator + ring producer.\nlet mut msg = pool.alloc::<Msg>()?; // get a message\nmsg.val = 42;                       // fill it in place\nproducer.send(msg)?;                // consumes the BufSlot;\n                                    // only its index travels\n                                    // through the ring\n\n// Receiver: ring consumer + freer (other thread or process).\nlet desc = consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>()?;\nlet msg = pool.resolve::<Msg>(&desc)?; // zero-copy view\n//                  ... read msg ...\nmsg.free();     // buffer back to its pool\ndesc.release(); // ring slot free for the next descriptor\n```\n\nOne allocation's bytes are written once and never copied —\nnot by send, not by receive, not across the process\nboundary.",
  "new_string": "**What \"send\" means.** Two forms compose:\n\n- Moving the `BufSlot` itself (an ordinary Rust move;\n  `Send` lets it cross threads or a std channel).\n- The descriptor flow (0.7.0): register the pool in a\n  per-process `PoolRegistry`, convert the guard to an\n  8-byte `Desc` with `into_desc`, and send *that* through\n  a ring as an ordinary POD message; the receiver\n  `resolve`s it back to an owned guard. The payload stays\n  put in its pool buffer:\n\n```rust,ignore\n// Sender: pool allocator + ring producer.\nlet mut msg = pool.alloc::<Msg>()?;   // get a message\nmsg.seq = 42;                         // fill it in place\nlet desc = registry.into_desc(pool_id, msg)?; // guard -> Desc\nlet mut slot = producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>()?;\n*slot = desc;                         // 8 bytes, not the payload\nslot.commit();\n\n// Receiver: ring consumer + freer (another thread).\nlet slot = consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>()?;\nlet desc = *slot;\nslot.release();                       // ring slot free again\n// SAFETY: desc came from into_desc, arrived via the ring's\n// commit -> reserve handoff, resolved exactly once.\nlet msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }?;\n//                  ... read msg ...\nmsg.free();                           // buffer back to its pool\n```\n\nOne allocation's bytes are written once and never copied —\nnot by send, not by receive. (`resolve` is the one `unsafe`:\nvalidation rejects unknown ids / bad indices / wrong types,\nbut ownership uniqueness is the caller's promise. Paired\nsender/receiver endpoints that encapsulate it — and shrink\nthis to loan / send / recv — are the next cycle; see\nnotes/todo.md.)"
}
```

===
# Notes

This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.

By default there are chores/chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
The chores-NN files are numbered in sequence; as a chores file grows
large the next one is opened ([chores/chores-01.md](chores/chores-01.md)
is the active one), and earlier ones are closed and
kept for history.

Project design docs:

- [ring-buffer-design.md](ring-buffer-design.md) — the
  zero-copy ring buffer (requirements, layout, API,
  validation), kept in sync with `src/`.


Examples chore file:
```
# Chores-01.md
 
General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
how and why of a particular chore.

## Create a binary that lists jj info 

This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
and using `jj-lib`
```

## Workflow and conventions

Bot-facing workflow and conventions live in
[`../AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md):

- [Notes file conventions](../AGENTS.md#notes-file-conventions)
  — Todo format, Reference numbering, Notes references
```

Agreed — thoughts:

**The sizing argument holds up.** Looking at what each todo actually contains now that the design is settled: #1 is three `_with` methods + a spin policy fn + three `_spin` wrappers + tests + converting the demo's loops; #2 is two endpoint types + tests + shrinking the demo; #3 is demo-only. Each is comparable to the 0.6.1–0.6.3 commits, which were exactly this pattern — single-commit lightweight cycles, patch bump, own chores section, no In Progress ladder, no prep/close-out ceremony. The 0.7.0 cycle needed a ladder because it carried unsettled design; these three don't — their design already lives in the `Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies` subsection, so the chores sections just cite it.

**Versioning agrees too.** Pre-1.0 semver treats `0.7.x` → `0.7.y` as the compatible-additions lane, and all three are purely additive API/demo work. Staying in `0.7.x` also keeps the descriptor era cohesive — 0.8.0 deserves to mark something structural (cross-process, say). So: `0.7.1` wait policies, `0.7.2` endpoints, `0.7.3` batch demo, each a distinct chores `##` entry rather than riding under the 0.7.0 section.

**The escape hatch if I'm wrong about sizing:** #1 has one known implementation wrinkle (the ring guards borrow the endpoint, so a naive retry-loop-returning-the-guard fights the borrow checker; the likely fix is an internal availability probe, sound under SPSC since the peer's progress only ever helps). If that or anything else balloons, versioning.md explicitly allows a lightweight cycle to grow a ladder after the fact — `0.7.1-1`, `-2` — without renumbering. So starting lightweight costs nothing.

**What I'd adjust from the edits I just staged:** version becomes `0.7.1` (not `0.8.0-0`), no In Progress block, and the `## feat: wait-policy hook + spin models` chores section I opened stays — it's exactly the "own chore entry" you're describing. The `[[28]]` close-out backfill also stays; that's correct regardless.

1	//! Producing endpoint: [`Producer`] reserves the next free
2	//! slot, the [`WriteSlot`] guard writes it in place and
3	//! commits.
4	
5	use core::marker::PhantomData;
6	use core::ops::{Deref, DerefMut};
7	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
8	use zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
9	
10	use crate::{Header, USER_WORDS, check_type, slot_ptr};
11	
12	/// `reserve_slot` failed: every slot holds an
13	/// uncommitted-or-unread message.
14	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
15	pub struct Full;
16	
17	/// The producing endpoint: `reserve_slot`, write in place,
18	/// `commit`.
19	pub struct Producer<'a> {
20	    /// The ring's control block.
21	    header: &'a Header,
22	    /// Base of the slot array.
23	    slots: *mut u8,
24	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
25	    slot_size: u32,
26	    /// Geometry snapshot (see [`Ring`](crate::Ring)).
27	    capacity: u32,
28	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
29	    mask: u32,
30	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
31	}
32	
33	// SAFETY: the handle owns the producer role; the shared state it
34	// touches (indices) is atomic, and slot writes are handed off
35	// with Release/Acquire ordering.
36	unsafe impl Send for Producer<'_> {}
37	
38	impl<'a> Producer<'a> {
39	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s
40	    /// geometry snapshot.
41	    pub(crate) fn new(
42	        header: &'a Header,
43	        slots: *mut u8,
44	        slot_size: u32,
45	        capacity: u32,
46	        mask: u32,
47	    ) -> Self {
48	        Producer {
49	            header,
50	            slots,
51	            slot_size,
52	            capacity,
53	            mask,
54	            _region: PhantomData,
55	        }
56	    }
57	
58	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
59	    /// never touched by the crate again.
60	    ///
61	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
62	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
63	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
64	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
65	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
66	        &self.header.user
67	    }
68	
69	    /// Reserve the next free slot as a `&mut T`, or [`Full`].
70	    ///
71	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
72	    ///   holds the `&mut Producer` borrow, so a second
73	    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or
74	    ///   committed does not compile.
75	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`WriteSlot::commit`]
76	    ///   abandons the reservation (nothing published).
77	    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
78	    where
79	        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,
80	    {
81	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
82	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
83	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
84	        // `>=`, not `==`: a peer-corrupted consumer_idx makes
85	        // occupancy look huge — fail Full, never hand out a
86	        // slot the protocol doesn't own.
87	        if p.wrapping_sub(c) >= self.capacity {
88	            return Err(Full);
89	        }
90	        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T`: a reference field would be
91	        // argument-protected for the whole `commit(self)` call,
92	        // while commit's Release store lets the consumer read
93	        // the slot inside that window — UB (Miri-verified).
94	        // Deref mints short-lived references instead.
95	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;
96	        Ok(WriteSlot {
97	            header: self.header,
98	            msg,
99	            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),
100	            _slot: PhantomData,
101	        })
102	    }
103	}
104	
105	/// A reserved write slot: `DerefMut` to write the message, then
106	/// [`commit`](WriteSlot::commit).
107	pub struct WriteSlot<'p, T> {
108	    /// The ring's control block (for the commit store).
109	    header: &'p Header,
110	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
111	    /// see the comment in [`Producer::reserve_slot`].
112	    msg: *mut T,
113	    /// Value `producer_idx` takes on commit.
114	    next_idx: u32,
115	    /// Owns the `&'p mut` borrow of the producer.
116	    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,
117	}
118	
119	impl<T> Deref for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
120	    type Target = T;
121	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
122	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
123	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
124	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
125	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot), and the index
126	        // protocol gives this guard exclusive slot access until
127	        // commit.
128	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
129	    }
130	}
131	
132	impl<T> DerefMut for WriteSlot<'_, T> {
133	    /// Write access to the in-slot message.
134	    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {
135	        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of
136	        // the minted reference.
137	        unsafe { &mut *self.msg }
138	    }
139	}
140	
141	impl<T> WriteSlot<'_, T> {
142	    /// Publish the slot to the consumer
143	    /// (`producer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
144	    pub fn commit(self) {
145	        self.header
146	            .producer_idx
147	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
148	    }
149	}
150

34	    /// Build the handle from [`Ring::split`](crate::Ring::split)'s

37	        Consumer {
38	            header,
39	            slots,
40	            slot_size,
41	            mask,
42	            _region: PhantomData,
43	        }
44	    }
45	
46	    /// The header's app-owned scratch line: zeroed at init,
47	    /// never touched by the crate again.
48	    ///
49	    /// - Shared with the peer: treat contents as untrusted
50	    ///   data — store values, never addresses to dereference.
51	    /// - See the design doc's "Blocking and user words" for
52	    ///   the wakeup-protocol contract it exists to host.
53	    pub fn user(&self) -> &[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS] {
54	        &self.header.user
55	    }
56	
57	    /// Reserve the oldest unread slot as a `&T`, or [`Empty`].
58	    ///
59	    /// - Only one slot may be reserved at a time: the guard
60	    ///   holds the `&mut Consumer` borrow, so a second
61	    ///   `reserve_slot` before the guard is dropped or released
62	    ///   does not compile.
63	    /// - Dropping the guard without [`ReadSlot::release`]
64	    ///   leaves the slot unread — the next `reserve_slot`
65	    ///   returns it again.
66	    pub fn reserve_slot<T>(&mut self) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>
67	    where
68	        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,
69	    {
70	        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);
71	        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
72	        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);
73	        if p == c {
74	            return Err(Empty);
75	        }
76	        // Raw pointer, not `&T`, for the same reason as in the
77	        // producer's reserve_slot: release's store frees the
78	        // slot for producer writes while a reference field
79	        // would still be argument-protected.
80	        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, c, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *const T;
81	        Ok(ReadSlot {
82	            header: self.header,
83	            msg,
84	            next_idx: c.wrapping_add(1),
85	            _slot: PhantomData,
86	        })
87	    }
88	}

90	/// A reserved read slot: `Deref` to read the message, then

93	    /// The ring's control block (for the release store).
94	    header: &'c Header,
95	    /// The slot, viewed as the message type. Raw on purpose —
96	    /// see the comment in [`Consumer::reserve_slot`].
97	    msg: *const T,
98	    /// Value `consumer_idx` takes on release.
99	    next_idx: u32,
100	    /// Owns the `&'c` borrow of the consumer.
101	    _slot: PhantomData<&'c T>,
102	}
103	
104	impl<T> Deref for ReadSlot<'_, T> {
105	    type Target = T;
106	    /// Read access to the in-slot message.
107	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
108	        // SAFETY: msg is in-bounds and aligned (check_type +
109	        // cache-line slot base), any byte pattern is a valid T
110	        // (FromBytes bound at reserve_slot), and the index
111	        // protocol gives this guard read access until release.
112	        unsafe { &*self.msg }
113	    }
114	}
115	
116	impl<T> ReadSlot<'_, T> {
117	    /// Free the slot for reuse (`consumer_idx + 1`, `Release`).
118	    pub fn release(self) {
119	        self.header
120	            .consumer_idx
121	            .store(self.next_idx, Ordering::Release);
122	    }

356:mod tests {
///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).
fn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {
    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;
    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot
    // array validated at init/attach.
    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }
}

#[cfg(test)]

[`Producer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Producer::reserve_slot_with),\n//! [`Consumer::reserve_slot_with`](crate::Consumer::reserve_slot_with))\n//! call it after each failed attempt with the attempt count\n//! (0-based, saturating); returning `false` gives up and the\n//! operation returns its usual error.\n//!\n//! - The crate ships only the policies its own demo and\n//!   tests use; compose your own (bounded retries, deadline,\n//!   spin-then-yield) as capturing closures at the call\n//!   site, or bake one into your own wrapper method.\n//! - Policies are polling strategies. True blocking (futex,\n//!   eventfd, async wakers) needs a peer wake over the\n//!   header's user line and is a layer above this crate.\n\n/// Spin forever: hint the CPU and keep waiting.\npub fn spin(_attempt: u32) -> bool {\n    core::hint::spin_loop();\n    true\n}\n"
}

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/pool.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// Buffer size in bytes (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_size(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.buf_size\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// [`alloc`](Pool::alloc) with an injected wait policy:\n    /// retry until a buffer frees up or the policy gives up.\n    ///\n    /// - `wait` is called after each failed attempt with the\n    ///   attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Exhausted)`.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn alloc_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, Exhausted>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        loop {\n            match self.alloc::<T>() {\n                Ok(buf_slot) => return Ok(buf_slot),\n                Err(Exhausted) => {\n                    if !wait(attempt) {\n                        return Err(Exhausted);\n                    }\n                    attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// [`alloc`](Pool::alloc), spinning until a buffer is\n    /// available — [`alloc_with`](Pool::alloc_with) under the\n    /// never-quitting [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin),\n    /// so no `Result`.\n    pub fn alloc_spin<T>(&mut self) -> BufSlot<'a, T>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        match self.alloc_with(crate::policy::spin) {\n            Ok(buf_slot) => buf_slot,\n            Err(Exhausted) => unreachable!(\"policy::spin never gives up\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Buffer size in bytes (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_size(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.buf_size\n    }"
}
