  "old_string": "        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;\n        Ok(WriteSlot {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),\n            _slot: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;\n        Ok(WriteSlot {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),\n            _slot: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot) with an\n    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or\n    /// the policy gives up.\n    ///\n    /// - `wait` is called after each failed attempt with the\n    ///   attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`.\n    /// - Waits on a probe, then reserves once: the guard\n    ///   borrows the endpoint, so a retry loop cannot return\n    ///   it. Sound under SPSC — between a not-full probe and\n    ///   the reserve only the consumer moves, which only\n    ///   frees slots (and `reserve_slot` re-validates anyway).\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        while self.is_full() {\n            if !wait(attempt) {\n                return Err(Full);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        self.reserve_slot()\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot), spinning\n    /// until a slot is available —\n    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Producer::reserve_slot_with)\n    /// under the never-quitting\n    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.\n    ///\n    /// A hostile peer scribbling `consumer_idx` can still\n    /// surface as a panic here (the post-probe reserve fails\n    /// toward `Full`); use `reserve_slot_with` where that\n    /// must degrade to an error instead.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> WriteSlot<'_, T>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {\n            Ok(slot) => slot,\n            Err(Full) => unreachable!(\"policy::spin never gives up\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// The occupancy probe `reserve_slot` starts with —\n    /// `true` when every slot holds an uncommitted-or-unread\n    /// message (or a peer-corrupted index makes it look so).\n    fn is_full(&self) -> bool {\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        p.wrapping_sub(c) >= self.capacity\n    }\n}"
}

  "new_string": "            s.spawn(move || {\n                for i in 0..COUNT {\n                    let b = rx.recv().unwrap();\n                    assert_eq!(b.seq, i);\n                    assert_eq!(b.val, i * 3);\n                    b.free(); // the freer role: not the allocator\n                }\n            });\n        });\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn alloc_with_policy_counts_and_gives_up() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut r.0, TEST_CACHE_LINE_SIZE, 4).unwrap();\n        // Drain the pool so alloc_with must wait.\n        let held: [_; 4] = core::array::from_fn(|_| pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap());\n\n        // The policy sees 0-based attempt counts and its\n        // `false` becomes Err(Exhausted).\n        let mut seen = Vec::new();\n        let err = pool\n            .alloc_with::<Msg>(|attempt| {\n                seen.push(attempt);\n                attempt < 2\n            })\n            .err()\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(err, Exhausted);\n        assert_eq!(seen, [0, 1, 2]);\n\n        // A freed buffer ends the wait with Ok.\n        let mut freed = false;\n        let mut held = held.into_iter();\n        let buf_slot = pool\n            .alloc_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                if !freed {\n                    held.next().unwrap().free();\n                    freed = true;\n                }\n                true\n            })\n            .unwrap();\n        buf_slot.free();\n        held.for_each(BufSlot::free);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn alloc_spin_returns_directly() {\n        let mut r = Region::new();\n        let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut r.0, TEST_CACHE_LINE_SIZE, 4).unwrap();\n        let mut buf_slot = pool.alloc_spin::<Msg>();\n        buf_slot.seq = 7;\n        assert_eq!(buf_slot.seq, 7);\n        buf_slot.free();\n    }\n}"
}

554:    fn threaded_spsc() {
        // Commit one, reserve_slot then drop: still unread, the
        // next reserve_slot returns it again.
        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
        slot.seq = 1;
        slot.commit();
        let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
        drop(msg);
        let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
        msg.release();
    }

    #[test]
    fn threaded_spsc() {
        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow,
        // and its scheduler explores interleavings at any count.
        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };
        let mut r = Region::new();
        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();

        std::thread::scope(|s| {
            s.spawn(move || {
                for i in 0..COUNT {
                    loop {
                        match prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
                            Ok(mut slot) => {
                                slot.seq = i;
                                slot.val = i * 3;
                                slot.commit();
                                break;
                            }
                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
                        }
                    }
                }
            });
            s.spawn(move || {
                for i in 0..COUNT {
                    loop {
                        match cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
                            Ok(msg) => {
                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
                                msg.release();
                                break;
                            }
                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
                        }
                    }
                }
            });
        });
    }
}

206	/// Move COUNT messages producer-thread → consumer-thread
207	/// through the ring; return elapsed seconds.
208	///
209	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
210	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
211	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
212	fn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
213	    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
214	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
215	        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
216	        .split();
217	
218	    let start = Instant::now();
219	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
220	        s.spawn(move || {
221	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
222	                pin_to_cpu(p);
223	            }
224	            for i in 0..COUNT {
225	                loop {
226	                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
227	                        Ok(mut slot) => {
228	                            slot.seq = i;
229	                            slot.commit();
230	                            break;
231	                        }
232	                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
233	                    }
234	                }
235	            }
236	        });
237	        s.spawn(move || {
238	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
239	                pin_to_cpu(c);
240	            }
241	            for i in 0..COUNT {
242	                loop {
243	                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
244	                        Ok(msg) => {

313	/// cross the SPSC ring, the consumer resolves and frees;
314	/// return elapsed seconds.
315	///
316	/// - `pin` — `Some((p, c))` pins the producer to cpu `p` and
317	///   the consumer to cpu `c`; `None` lets the scheduler place
318	///   them (the number then depends on where they land).
319	fn spsc_ring_one_pool_msg_2t(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {
320	    let mut ring_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
321	    let mut pool_region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);
322	    let mut pool = Pool::init(&mut pool_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH).unwrap(); // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the pool header + DEPTH buffers
323	    let mut registry = PoolRegistry::<1>::new();
324	    let pool_id = registry.register(pool.resolver()).unwrap(); // OK: empty capacity-1 registry always has room
325	    let registry = &registry;
326	    let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
327	        Ring::init(&mut ring_region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)
328	            .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots
329	            .split();
330	
331	    let start = Instant::now();
332	    std::thread::scope(|s| {
333	        s.spawn(move || {
334	            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {
335	                pin_to_cpu(p);
336	            }
337	            for i in 0..COUNT {
338	                let mut buf_slot = loop {
339	                    match pool.alloc::<Msg>() {
340	                        Ok(buf_slot) => break buf_slot,
341	                        Err(Exhausted) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
342	                    }
343	                };
344	                buf_slot.seq = i;
345	                let desc = registry
346	                    .into_desc(pool_id, buf_slot)
347	                    .map_err(|(_, e)| e)
348	                    .unwrap(); // OK: pool_id came from this registry's register
349	                loop {
350	                    match producer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
351	                        Ok(mut slot) => {
352	                            *slot = desc;
353	                            slot.commit();
354	                            break;
355	                        }
356	                        Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
357	                    }
358	                }
359	            }
360	        });
361	        s.spawn(move || {
362	            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {
363	                pin_to_cpu(c);
364	            }
365	            for i in 0..COUNT {
366	                let desc = loop {
367	                    match consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>() {
368	                        Ok(slot) => {
369	                            let desc = *slot;
370	                            slot.release();
371	                            break desc;
372	                        }
373	                        Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
374	                    }
375	                };
376	                // SAFETY: the desc was consumed into the ring
377	                // by the producer and read after the commit →
378	                // reserve handoff (happens-before); each is
379	                // resolved exactly once.
380	                let msg = unsafe { registry.resolve::<Msg>(desc) }.unwrap(); // OK: descs here only come from the producer's into_desc
381	                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
382	                msg.free();
383	            }
384	        });
385	    });
386	    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()
387	}

  "new_string": "## feat: wait-policy hook + spin models\n\nCommits:\n\nTodo #1: every wait in the demo and tests was a hand-rolled\nspin loop — nine lines of ceremony per wait point. The hook\nmakes the wait an injected policy (`FnMut(u32) -> bool`:\nattempt count in, keep-waiting out) and ships the one policy\nwe dogfood, per the tier model in\n[Follow-on: endpoints and wait policies](#follow-on-endpoints-and-wait-policies).\n\n- `_with` variants on `Pool::alloc` and both endpoints'\n  `reserve_slot`; the policy is called after each failed\n  attempt (0-based, saturating count), `false` returns the\n  operation's usual error.\n- The endpoints wait on an occupancy probe, then reserve\n  once — the guard borrows the endpoint, so a retry loop\n  cannot return it. Sound under SPSC: between a good probe\n  and the reserve only the peer moves, and its progress\n  only helps; a hostile scribble in that window surfaces as\n  the ordinary error (documented on both `_with`s), never a\n  wrong reservation.\n- `policy::spin` is the only shipped policy; the `_spin`\n  wrappers return the value directly (no `Result` — spin\n  never quits), turning the corrupted-ring case into a\n  labeled panic.\n- Demo waits converted to the `_spin` models (the 2t loops\n  shrank to the target shape); the 1t impossible-state\n  panics stay raw matches — they are assertions, not waits.\n- Remaining clause of the todo — measure raw loop vs\n  closure vs `_spin` in iiac-perf to verify the seam is\n  zero-cost — happens in the sibling repo.\n\n# References"
}

> AGENT

`0.7.1` is complete and validated — ready for your review. What's in the working copy:

**New API:**
- `policy` module — the wait-policy contract documented in one place, shipping only `spin` (the model users copy; the module doc records the composition tiers and the polling-vs-true-blocking boundary).
- `Pool::alloc_with(policy)` / `alloc_spin()` — simple retry (the guard doesn't borrow the pool); `_spin` returns `BufSlot` directly, no `Result`, since spin never quits.
- `Producer::reserve_slot_with` / `reserve_slot_spin`, `Consumer::` same — the probe-then-reserve-once shape, since ring guards borrow the endpoint. Doc comments carry the SPSC soundness argument and one honest caveat found while writing it: a hostile peer scribbling an index between probe and reserve can produce `Err(Full/Empty)` even under a never-quitting policy (callers treating `Err` as backpressure stay correct; `_spin` turns that corruption case into a labeled panic, not a mislabeled `unreachable!`).

**Tests** (28 total, +4): policies see 0-based attempt counts and their `false` becomes the right error (pool and both endpoints); policies are *never consulted* when the operation can succeed immediately; a mid-wait `free` ends `alloc_with` with `Ok`; and a threaded `_spin` roundtrip mirroring `threaded_spsc`. Full suite clean under Miri (10.7s — the `cfg!(miri)` count reduction from last cycle paying off).

**Demo converted** — every 2t wait loop is now the 3-line `_spin` form (`let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>(); slot.seq = i; slot.commit();`); the 1t impossible-state panics stay raw matches since they're assertions, not waits. Demo numbers look consistent with previous runs (this run was globally a bit slow — even `global_alloc` drifted — so no regression signal; the real zero-cost verdict is the iiac-perf item).

**Notes:** chores section filled (cites the Follow-on design subsection), Done entry added, and Todo #1 replaced by its remaining clause — the iiac-perf three-way measurement. The `[[28]]` close-out backfill from earlier is also in this diff.

Ready for the commit description?

> DEVELOPER

Looking at it you removed the "raw" versions, I think we should keep them and add _closure and the _spin that uses closure, thoughts only.

> AGENT

Good catch, and I agree with the instinct — my conversion destroyed the raw baseline in the very repo that's supposed to feed the three-way comparison. Thoughts:

**Why raw must stay:** it's the reference implementation of "today's floor" — the thing iiac-perf ports first, the thing `_with`/`_spin` are measured against, and the honest picture of the primitive API. Converting every demo loop to `_spin` also quietly changed what the demo measures right before we ask "is the seam zero-cost?" — exactly the wrong moment to lose the control group.

**But three forms everywhere would explode the scoreboard.** Every 2t family × 3 placements × 3 forms ≈ 27 ring-family lines. So rather than converting or tripling everything, I'd structure it as: **all existing functions revert to raw** (status quo ante), plus a **dedicated three-way exemplar** of one family — the raw ring `one_msg`, since it's the purest wait-dominated loop:

```
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t (same core 0+12):        raw
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_with (same core 0+12):   closure via reserve_slot_with
spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_spin (same core 0+12):   _spin wrappers
```

I'd run the three-way at **same-core only** (+ optionally the 1t): same-core is the most repeatable cross-thread placement, and the question is form-vs-form at *identical* placement, so one placement carries the signal. The 1t three-way is interesting for a different reason — there the ring is never Full/Empty, the policy is never consulted, so it isolates the probe's pure call overhead with no waiting at all. Together: +4 output lines, not +18.

**Expectation-setting caveat:** the demo's single-run noise (we've watched ±30% between runs) cannot resolve a few-ns seam difference — adjacent same-run lines share thermal/placement conditions so the comparison is better than cross-run, but the verdict still belongs to iiac-perf's calibrated mean/stdev. The demo three-way is the eyeball sanity check (catches "grossly wrong," not "2% slower") and, equally valuable, keeps all three forms as living, compiling code models side by side in one file.

**Dogfooding note:** with the demo reverted to raw elsewhere, `_with`/`_spin` stay dogfooded via the exemplar pair plus the tests — the "ship only what we use" rule holds.

Want me to do that — revert the conversions, add `spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_with`/`_spin` at same-core (and the 1t pair if you want it)?

> DEVELOPER

Yes, do that including the 1t pair and as it performance is significantly different from all the 2t's and it might show greater disparity if there is any

> DEVELOPER

Yes, do that including the 1t pair and as it performance is significantly different from all the 2t's and it might show greater disparity if there is any.

> AGENT

Right — at 2.6 ns/iteration the 1t loop makes any per-op overhead visible as a percentage where the 2t's coherence noise would bury it. Implementing: revert the three conversions to raw, then add the four exemplar functions.

size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut slot = producer\n                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_with: producer Full SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\")\n                    })\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: the policy panics instead of giving up\n                slot.seq = i;\n                slot.commit();\n                let msg = consumer\n                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                        panic!(\"spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_with: consumer Empty SHOULD NOT HAPPEN\")\n                    })\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: the policy panics instead of giving up\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// spsc_ring_one_msg_1t through `reserve_slot_spin` — the\n/// shipped-model form of the three-way seam check; like the\n/// raw 1t it never actually waits.\nfn spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_spin() -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            pin_to_cpu(0);\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                slot.seq = i;\n                slot.commit();\n                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// spsc_ring_one_msg_2t through `reserve_slot_with` with a\n/// spinning closure — the closure form of the three-way seam\n/// check under real waiting.\nfn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_with(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(p);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut slot = producer\n                    .reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(|_| {\n                        std::hint::spin_loop();\n                        true\n                    })\n                    .unwrap(); // OK: the closure never gives up\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// spsc_ring_one_msg_2t through `reserve_slot_spin` — the\n/// shipped-model form of the three-way seam check under real\n/// waiting.\nfn spsc_ring_one_msg_2t_spin(pin: PinPair) -> f64 {\n    let mut region = Region([0; size_of::<Region>()]);\n    let (mut producer, mut consumer) = Ring::init(&mut region.0, CACHE_LINE_SIZE as u32, DEPTH)\n        .unwrap() // OK: Region is sized/aligned for the ring header + DEPTH slots\n        .split();\n\n    let start = Instant::now();\n    std::thread::scope(|s| {\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((p, _)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(p);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                slot.seq = i;\n                slot.commit();\n            }\n        });\n        s.spawn(move || {\n            if let Some((_, c)) = pin {\n                pin_to_cpu(c);\n            }\n            for i in 0..COUNT {\n                let msg = consumer.reserve_slot_spin::<Msg>();\n                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);\n                msg.release();\n            }\n        });\n    });\n    start.elapsed().as_secs_f64()\n}\n\n/// The composed flow on one thread pinned to cpu 0: one pool\n/// message allocated outside the timed loop; each iteration"

{

  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/zc-ring-x1/src/producer.rs",
  "old_string": "    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot) with an\n    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or\n    /// the policy gives up.\n    ///\n    /// - `wait` is called after each failed attempt with the\n    ///   attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`.\n    /// - Waits on a probe, then reserves once: the guard\n    ///   borrows the endpoint, so a retry loop cannot return\n    ///   it. Sound under SPSC — between a not-full probe and\n    ///   the reserve only the consumer moves, which only\n    ///   frees slots (and `reserve_slot` re-validates anyway).\n    /// - A hostile peer scribbling `consumer_idx` between\n    ///   probe and reserve can produce `Err(Full)` even\n    ///   under a never-quitting policy — callers treating\n    ///   `Err` as backpressure stay correct.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        while self.is_full() {\n            if !wait(attempt) {\n                return Err(Full);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        self.reserve_slot()\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot), spinning\n    /// until a slot is available —\n    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Producer::reserve_slot_with)\n    /// under the never-quitting\n    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.\n    ///\n    /// A hostile peer scribbling `consumer_idx` can still\n    /// surface as a panic here (the post-probe reserve fails\n    /// toward `Full`); use `reserve_slot_with` where that\n    /// must degrade to an error instead.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> WriteSlot<'_, T>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {\n            Ok(slot) => slot,\n            Err(Full) => panic!(\"reserve failed after not-full probe: peer-corrupted ring\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// The occupancy probe `reserve_slot` starts with —\n    /// `true` when every slot holds an uncommitted-or-unread\n    /// message (or a peer-corrupted index makes it look so).\n    fn is_full(&self) -> bool {\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        p.wrapping_sub(c) >= self.capacity\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot) with an\n    /// injected wait policy: retry until a slot frees up or\n    /// the policy gives up.\n    ///\n    /// - `wait` is called after each failed attempt with the\n    ///   attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning\n    ///   `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`.\n    /// - The wait loop is the reservation itself (the guard\n    ///   borrows the endpoint, so retrying a `reserve_slot`\n    ///   call could not return it): `producer_idx` is ours\n    ///   and loaded once; only `consumer_idx` is re-read per\n    ///   attempt, so the no-wait fast path does exactly the\n    ///   loads `reserve_slot` does.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        // Ours alone: no peer moves it, load once.\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        loop {\n            // Same load + `>=` occupancy check as\n            // reserve_slot (see there for the corruption\n            // rationale).\n            let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n            if p.wrapping_sub(c) < self.capacity {\n                break;\n            }\n            if !wait(attempt) {\n                return Err(Full);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        // Raw pointer, not `&mut T` — see reserve_slot.\n        let msg = slot_ptr(self.slots, p, self.mask, self.slot_size) as *mut T;\n        Ok(WriteSlot {\n            header: self.header,\n            msg,\n            next_idx: p.wrapping_add(1),\n            _slot: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Producer::reserve_slot), spinning\n    /// until a slot is available —\n    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Producer::reserve_slot_with)\n    /// under the never-quitting\n    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> WriteSlot<'_, T>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {\n            Ok(slot) => slot,\n            Err(Full) => unreachable!(\"policy::spin never gives up\"),\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

Sound under SPSC — between a not-empty probe and\n    ///   the reserve only the producer moves, which only adds\n    ///   messages (and `reserve_slot` re-validates anyway).\n    /// - A hostile peer scribbling `producer_idx` between\n    ///   probe and reserve can produce `Err(Empty)` even\n    ///   under a never-quitting policy — callers treating\n    ///   `Err` as \"nothing yet\" stay correct.\n    /// - See [`policy`](crate::policy) for shipped policies\n    ///   and the composition model.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(\n        &mut self,\n        mut wait: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,\n    ) -> Result<ReadSlot<'_, T>, Empty>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.slot_size);\n        let mut attempt = 0u32;\n        while self.is_empty() {\n            if !wait(attempt) {\n                return Err(Empty);\n            }\n            attempt = attempt.saturating_add(1);\n        }\n        self.reserve_slot()\n    }\n\n    /// [`reserve_slot`](Consumer::reserve_slot), spinning\n    /// until a message arrives —\n    /// [`reserve_slot_with`](Consumer::reserve_slot_with)\n    /// under the never-quitting\n    /// [`policy::spin`](crate::policy::spin), so no `Result`.\n    ///\n    /// A hostile peer scribbling `producer_idx` can still\n    /// surface as a panic here (the post-probe reserve fails\n    /// toward `Empty`); use `reserve_slot_with` where that\n    /// must degrade to an error instead.\n    pub fn reserve_slot_spin<T>(&mut self) -> ReadSlot<'_, T>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + KnownLayout + Immutable,\n    {\n        match self.reserve_slot_with(crate::policy::spin) {\n            Ok(slot) => slot,\n            Err(Empty) => panic!(\"reserve failed after not-empty probe: peer-corrupted ring\"),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// The occupancy probe `reserve_slot` starts with —\n    /// `true` when no unread message is published.\n    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {\n        let c = self.header.consumer_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let p = self.header.producer_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n        p == c\n    }\n}",

> AGENT

All validated — ready for your review. The revised `0.7.1` shape:

**API (as before, plus the fix the exemplar forced):** `policy::spin` (now `#[inline]` — it wasn't cross-crate-inlining into the monomorphized `_with`), `Pool::alloc_with/_spin`, and endpoint `reserve_slot_with/_spin` where the wait loop now **is** the reservation — own index loaded once, peer index re-read per attempt — so the no-wait fast path does exactly the loads raw `reserve_slot` does. The probe-then-reserve draft and its "hostile scribble between probe and reserve" caveat are gone; `_spin`'s error arm is a legitimate `unreachable!` again.

**Demo:** every pre-existing loop is back to raw (the baseline), plus the four-function three-way exemplar — `spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_with/_1t_spin` (policy asserts instead of waiting; measures pure per-call overhead) and `_2t_with/_2t_spin` at same-core (overhead under real waiting), slotted adjacent to their raw siblings.

**The story worth the price of admission** — the exemplar caught two real regressions in an API whose whole pitch is "zero-cost":

| form | first measurement | after fixes |
|---|---|---|
| raw 1t | 2.8 ns | 2.8 ns |
| `_with` 1t | 6.2 ns | 2.9 ns |
| `_spin` 1t | 10.2 ns | 3.3 ns |

Same-core 2t tells the same story (raw ~8, `_with` 6.8, `_spin` 7.1 — now within run noise of each other). The chores narrative records both regressions and their fixes; the iiac-perf item remains as the calibrated confirmation.

fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, 28/28 tests, Miri clean, installed binary reports 0.7.1. Ready for the commit description?

> DEVELOPER

In above you used the work exemplar, new word for me that I had to lookup:
 "a person or thing serving as a typical or excellent model"

I like it and it's a perfect word, but I don't want others to have to look it up.
Either provide a link to the definition or maybe "the raw model" meaning the
highest performing version that we know of for that shape. Even "raw model" needs
to be defined so I suggest we define "exemplar" as "the highest ..".

Thoughts?

> AGENT

Good catch, and it maps onto a distinction worth keeping — because "exemplar" and "raw" are actually two different things in that sentence, and your proposed definition describes the second:

- **raw** — the hand-rolled loop: the baseline, the fastest form we know for that shape, what the others are measured against.
- **exemplar** — the *family shown in all three forms side by side*: raw, `_with(closure)`, `_spin` for one workload. It's the worked model of the comparison itself, not the fastest member of it.

So defining "exemplar" as "the highest performing version" would quietly merge the two, and then we'd need a new word for the comparison family anyway.

On mechanics, I'd follow the same principle as the speculation marker: don't make the reader decode; define at first use, in place. A parenthetical costs one line:

> …adds a three-way seam **exemplar** — one workload written in all three forms side by side, the worked model of the comparison — with **raw** (the hand-rolled loop, the fastest form we know for the shape) as its baseline…

A dictionary link would send the reader *out* of the doc for less information than the parenthetical gives them, since the generic definition doesn't say what it means *here*. And a glossary section feels heavy for one word — though there's precedent (versioning.md's `## Terms`), so if project vocabulary keeps accumulating (raw, seam, exemplar, ladder…), promoting to a Terms section later is the natural move, same opportunistic rule as reference re-packing.

Want me to fold that definition into the chores bullet (first use), and echo the raw/exemplar pairing in the demo's module doc where the four functions live? Both are in the uncommitted working copy, so it rides in this commit.

> AGENT

Folded in — the chores bullet now defines both terms at first use (as sub-bullets, per prose form), and the demo module doc carries the same raw/`_with`/`_spin` explanation where the exemplar functions live. fmt and clippy stay clean.

**Proposed commit description:**

```
feat: wait-policy hook + spin models

Waits become an injected policy (FnMut(u32) -> bool: attempt
count in, keep-waiting out): _with variants on pool alloc
and both endpoints' reserve_slot, policy::spin as the one
shipped model, _spin wrappers returning the value directly.
The demo gains a three-way seam exemplar (raw / closure /
_spin) that caught two real regressions before landing;
after fixes the 1t ladder reads 2.8 / 2.9 / 3.3 ns.

- src/policy.rs (new): wait-policy contract + spin
  (#[inline]: non-generic, must cross-crate-inline into
  monomorphized _with)
- src/producer.rs, src/consumer.rs: reserve_slot_with —
  the wait loop is the reservation (own index loaded once,
  peer index re-read per attempt: no-wait fast path does
  exactly reserve_slot's loads); reserve_slot_spin
- src/pool.rs: alloc_with (simple retry; BufSlot does not
  borrow the pool), alloc_spin
- src/lib.rs: pub mod policy; wait-policy tests (attempt
  counts, give-up errors, policy never consulted when
  ready, threaded _spin roundtrip)
- src/bin/zc-ring-x1-demo.rs: existing loops stay raw;
  spsc_ring_one_msg_{1t,2t}_{with,spin} exemplar lines
  (1t: pure per-call overhead; same-core 2t: overhead
  under real waiting); module doc defines raw/exemplar
- notes/chores/chores-01.md: section filled (terms defined
  at first use, the two caught regressions recorded);
  [[28]] backfill for the 0.7.0 close-out
- notes/todo.md: #1 becomes its measurement clause
  (iiac-perf three-way); Done entry
- Cargo.toml: version 0.7.1

> AGENT

Thoughts — I'd split the diagnosis by *where* the head-scratch happens, because I think it's real in one place and not the other:

**At API call sites, `_with` answers its own question.** This is the std convention: `get_or_insert_with(f)`, `resize_with(f)`, `repeat_with(f)` — `_with` idiomatically means "with a function you supply," and every real call site completes the phrase: `reserve_slot_with(|n| n < 1000)`. Renaming the API to `reserve_slot_with_closure` would be redundant at the call site (the closure is visibly right there) and break the std-idiom scent that tells a Rust reader "closure parameter" at a glance. `_with_spin` has a subtler problem: `reserve_slot_spin` names *behavior* ("reserve, spinning"), while `reserve_slot_with_spin` names *implementation* ("via the hook, using the spin policy") — callers care about the former.

**But in the demo labels, you're right — it dangles.** `spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_with (core 0):` has no argument in sight, so "with what?" is exactly what a scoreboard reader asks. And notice the vocabulary we've naturally used in every prose discussion of the three-way: **raw / closure / `_spin`**. The demo function names should match the prose: `spsc_ring_one_msg_1t_closure`, `_2t_closure`. That fixes the dangling word precisely where it dangles, keeps the API idiomatic, and makes scoreboard, prose, and chores all speak the same three-word vocabulary. Cost: labels grow 3 chars, pad 43 → 46, lines still under 85 cols.

So my recommendation: API stays `_with`/`_spin`; demo exemplar functions and labels rename `_with` → `_closure`; `_spin` stays everywhere (as a label it doesn't dangle — "spinning" is a complete thought). One supporting argument for keeping the API name: when iiac-perf reports "closure form," it will be *calling* `reserve_slot_with` — the form-name and the method-name being different is fine, form names describe the caller's code, method names describe the callee.

If that lands for you, the demo rename is a two-minute change — say the word and it goes in as part of whatever we commit next (0.7.2 endpoints, or its own tiny cleanup commit if you prefer it not wait).

> AGENT

Sharp observation, and it does genuinely weaken my adjacency defense — at the actual call site the reader encounters `reserve_slot_with::<Msg>` as a completed phrase *before* the closure appears, so for a beat, "with... Msg?" is a plausible misreading. The turbofish photobombs the idiom.

Two things worth noting about it:

1. **It's the turbofish's fault, not the name's** — and the turbofish exists because `T` can't be inferred here (field access through the guard's `Deref` doesn't drive inference). Which means Todo #5 — typed endpoints, `Producer<T>`/`Consumer<T>` validating geometry once at split — would fix this as a side effect: `producer.reserve_slot_with(|_| ...)` with no turbofish, closure snug against `_with`, the idiom reading exactly as std intends. Your observation is quiet extra motivation for that entry when its turn comes.

2. **It also retroactively validates the demo rename** — the scoreboard label was the *worst* case (no argument at all), the turbofish site is the *middling* case (argument displaced), and plain inferred calls are the good case. The fix landed where the reading is worst, and the API name stays aligned with std for the cases std designed it for.

> DEVELOPER

Continuing the discussion, I'd argue that `producer.reserve_slot_closure::<Msg>(|_| ...` reads better. Although on the closure in general it's not obvious to me what the closure is supposed to do looking at a call site. It handles Empty/Full but you have no idea looking at the call site. Thoughts on that?

> AGENT

Two separate problems are tangled here, and they have different best fixes:

**On `reserve_slot_closure`:** it names the argument's *kind*, but knowing "this takeses a closure" is exactly what you just showed doesn't help — the reader still doesn't know what the closure decides. If we rename for meaning, name the *role*. The std precedent is `Condvar::wait_while(guard, condition)`: `reserve_slot_while(|n| n < 100)` reads "reserve the slot, waiting while the policy says so" — the method name itself tells you the closure is a keep-waiting condition. That's strictly more informative than either `_with` or `_closure`.

**On "you can't tell what the closure does at the call site" — that's the real finding, and it has two levers:**

1. **Named policies are the primary answer, and we already designed for it.** The tier model says anonymous closures at call sites are the *exception*: real code passes `policy::spin` or its own named `bounded_100`, and `reserve_slot_with(policy::spin)` is perfectly legible — the policy's name carries the meaning. The demo exemplar deliberately uses a bare closure (it's demonstrating the closure arm), which makes it the least-legible call site in the codebase by design. Tests and exemplars tolerate that; application code shouldn't write it.

2. **The `bool` is the deeper culprit — classic boolean blindness.** `true` meaning "keep waiting" is a convention you must have read the docs to know; `|_| { spin_loop(); true }` is opaque precisely because of it. The type-level fix is an expressive return — std's `ControlFlow` (`Continue(())` = keep waiting, `Break(())` = give up) or a tiny domain enum (`Wait::Again` / `Wait::GiveUp`). Then even an anonymous closure self-documents: `|n| if n < 100 { Wait::Again } else { Wait::GiveUp }`. The cost is real though: `|n| n < 100` is beautifully terse, and every policy gets wordier.

My honest weighing: the enum fixes legibility *at the site that shouldn't exist in real code* (anonymous closures), while named policies fix it everywhere at zero cost — so I'd keep `bool` + the named-policy culture for now, and treat `ControlFlow`/`Wait` as the upgrade if we ever catch confusion in practice (it's a mechanical, pre-1.0-cheap change). The rename to `reserve_slot_while` is the cheapest genuine improvement on the table if `_with` keeps itching — it encodes the closure's role in the method name, which survives turbofish, scoreboard labels, and prose alike.

Nothing changed — where do you want to land? Keep `_with` + bool as-is, take the `_while` rename, and/or the `Wait` enum?

> DEVELOPER

This closure is handling the "fail" case, it seems that should be in the name Producer::reserve_slot_closure_invoked_on_full and Consumer::reserve_slot_closure_invoked_on_empty now I know. If we name the Policy::on_empty and on_full then reserve_slot_closure_invoked(on_xxx). thoughts?

> AGENT

The core insight — *the closure's identity is "what happens on the fail case," so the fail case should be visible* — is right. The question is where that information lives cheapest. Ranking the options:

**1. Name the parameter `on_full` / `on_empty` / `on_exhausted` — high value, zero cost.** Signatures become `reserve_slot_with(&mut self, on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool)`. This shows up in three places readers actually look: the docs, the signature popup, and — the big one — **rust-analyzer inlay hints render the parameter name at the call site**, so in your editor the exemplar literally reads `reserve_slot_with(on_full: |_| …)`. That's your "now I know," delivered exactly where the confusion happens, with no API break (parameter names aren't part of the API) and no method-name growth.

**2. Event in the method name — possible, but watch the traps.** `reserve_slot_closure_invoked_on_full` says everything and is 35 characters of it; call sites and docs will grumble. The compressed forms have a subtle misread: `reserve_slot_when_full(f)` parses as "reserve the slot when full" — the opposite of what happens. Something like `reserve_slot_or_wait(on_full)` avoids that ("reserve, or wait — per this policy"), keeps Producer/Consumer method names symmetric, and pairs with the parameter naming from option 1. If the method name should carry the semantics, that's the shape I'd pick over `_closure_invoked_on_*`.

**3. Naming the *policies* `on_full`/`on_empty` — I'd push back on this one.** A policy is event-agnostic by design: `policy::spin` answers "how do I wait," and the same spin works for Full, Empty, and Exhausted. Naming policies after events would force three aliases of every policy and put the information on the wrong noun. The event belongs to the *call* (the parameter), the strategy belongs to the *policy* — `reserve_slot_with(on_full: policy::spin)` keeps each name carrying its own fact.

So my recommendation: **do option 1 now** (rename the three parameters; docs and inlay hints do the rest), consider `_or_wait` as the method name only if `_with` still itches after living with the param names, and leave policies event-neutral. Option 1 is small enough to ride in the pending `0.7.2` rename commit alongside the `_closure` demo functions.

All three renamed and validated. Here's what you now see, in each of the three places the name surfaces:

**The signature** (docs, hover, completion):
```rust
pub fn reserve_slot_with<T>(
    &mut self,
    mut on_full: impl FnMut(u32) -> bool,
) -> Result<WriteSlot<'_, T>, Full>
```

**The doc bullet** (each `_with` now leads with its event):
> `on_full` is called after each failed attempt with the attempt count (0-based, saturating); returning `false` gives up → `Err(Full)`.

**The call site in your editor** — rust-analyzer's inlay hints render the parameter name, so the exemplar line displays as:
```rust
producer.reserve_slot_with::<Msg>(on_full: |_| { … })
```
(In plain text like `git diff` the hint isn't there, of course — that's the one surface this doesn't reach; there, the method docs are one hover away.)

The trio: `Producer::reserve_slot_with(on_full: …)`, `Consumer::reserve_slot_with(on_empty: …)`, `Pool::alloc_with(on_exhausted: …)` — the method name stays symmetric and std-idiomatic, and each event lives on its own parameter. fmt, clippy, and all 28 tests clean.

Live with it in the editor for a moment if you like — if it lands, this rides in the `0.7.2` commit together with the demo `_closure` rename and the chores vocabulary echo. Say the word for the description.

> DEVELOPER

I like it, show me the description (although as a person that hasn't used a lot of closures I'd probably prefer _closure or _with_closure but renaming the parameter really does help!!!), Thoughts on _with vs _with_closure vs _closure?

> AGENT

My thoughts on the three, honestly weighed:

- **`_with`** — the std idiom (`get_or_insert_with`, `resize_with`, `wait_while`'s cousin family). Its whole value is *recognition*: a fluent Rust reader parses "closure parameter" without thinking. Its cost falls on closure-newcomers exactly once — and std itself will teach them the idiom soon anyway, because they'll meet `unwrap_or_else` and `get_or_insert_with` everywhere. With `on_full` now carrying the *meaning*, `_with`'s only remaining job is that recognition signal.
- **`_closure` / `_with_closure`** — both name the mechanism, and as you discovered yourself two messages ago: knowing it's a closure doesn't tell you what it decides. So the suffix spends 8–13 characters buying information the parameter name already delivers better. `_with_closure` additionally reads redundant to fluent readers ("with" already said that), and `reserve_slot_closure` is a noun pileup with no verb connecting it.

So my recommendation stands: **`_with` + `on_full`** — convention for the fluent reader, semantics for everyone. And a note for the record: if it still itches after living with it, the genuine upgrade isn't `_closure`, it's `reserve_slot_or_wait(on_full)` — naming the *behavior*. Pre-1.0, that rename stays cheap forever. (The demo keeping `_closure` for its *form names* is right regardless — there the word distinguishes which arm of the comparison you're looking at, which is exactly a mechanism distinction.)

Let me finish the bookkeeping this commit needs (chores vocabulary echo, new chores section, Done entry, version), then the description:
